madam president, i welcome the work of the rapporteurs who, on such a sensitive subject, have used their common sense to put the scientific evidence above the negative campaign based on false data, which many are trying to pass off as electoral gain. the epp does not indulge in these populist tendencies, but always stands by its constituents in defending their rights, in providing the best information available and in seeking the best solutions. the same applies to agriculture, which many people want to make out to be the sector responsible for the poor use of these products. this is totally false. not only are pesticides used by a wide range of people and professions, but farmers are the main stakeholders in their responsible use. i would remind you that the most recent study on the presence of pesticide residues in the agri-food sector showed that 97.2% of the samples in europe were within the legal limits of european union legislation, which is a testimony to a very strict food production system and the safest in the world. european citizens can therefore trust their institutions and the supervisory bodies. that is also what i have been able to put into the document for which i was responsible in the committee on agriculture. it is clear that there is always room for improvement and that is why i welcome the increase in funding, both for the european food safety authority and for the member states' authorities, so that these bodies can develop their work even better. that is why we are here, we are working to build up the confidence of europeans in our europe.
madam president, i welcome the reference in the report to the need to strengthen research and innovation in the field of crop protection. this should be our main approach: to seek new technical solutions and methodologies which will make it possible to reduce the use of plant protection products in a sustainable way and, where their use is necessary, to do so in the most efficient and targeted way, always with the principle of precaution and the protection of human, animal and environmental health as undisputed priorities. we will also have to ensure that farmers have the tools at their disposal to ensure that their crops are properly protected and that they are able to run their farms. as such, the authorisation system for these products in the european union, while always based on the most rigorous scientific criteria, should be an agile system capable of providing solutions to the great diversity of european agricultural production, particularly in more challenging situations such as those we find in the outermost regions.
madam president, the creation of this special committee on the authorization procedures for pesticides was the result of public suspicion about the rigor and transparency of these procedures. this suspicion did not fall from the sky. the promiscuity between the european commission, its agencies and the interests of industry, dominated by transnational giants, in the case of chemicals and pharmaceuticals, is well known. the principles of prevention and precaution are not always duly taken into account in the authorisation procedures. it is public health and the preservation of the environment that must prevail over the interests of industry, its profit expectations and not the other way around. risk analysis cannot be used as a justification for turning this basic principle on its head. this applies both to the necessary authorization for monitoring the impact of what already exists on the market, to the management of innovation processes and to the large-scale application of new products.
i would like to congratulate the romanian presidency on taking over the presidency and i would also ask it to support this dossier so that we can conclude it during this term of office. the proposal we are voting on tomorrow on the use of driverless rental vehicles in road freight transport - which is part of the first mobility package - aims to adapt a directive that has been in force for 25 years and thus gradually adapt the market for the use of these vehicles, adapting it, of course, to the needs of the industry and taking account of the very developments on the transport market. what we are proposing is that member states should not restrict the use of a rental vehicle by an undertaking established in another member state within their own territory, provided that the vehicle obviously meets the requirements and operational and safety standards. in this way we are unlocking the potential of a rental and leasing market which, as we know, is made up mainly of small and mediumsized enterprises and which obviously looks forward to the possibility of creating more jobs. however, in order to avoid market distortions, which are obviously due to the existence of different levels of taxation, this document provides for the possibility for member states to limit the use of vehicles hired by an undertaking established in another member state to their territory for a period of time or to the total number of vehicles hired, provided that it allows the use of this vehicle on its territory for at least four consecutive months or, on the other hand, to limit the number of vehicles hired to 25% of the total fleet of this undertaking. and if the fleet consists of more than one or less than four vehicles, authorise the rental of at least one vehicle. for the correct implementation of this legislative act, we propose that all information on the rental vehicle be placed in the national electronic registers and that the exchange of information between member states be carried out through the internal market information system, as the proposal is in other dossiers of the mobility package itself. this proposal will make it possible to achieve the single market in this specific area without doing so abruptly, and also by achieving compliance with the social and market rules, especially in the union's freight transport. i therefore appeal to all members not to support amendments which discriminate between rental and private vehicles and which create further obstacles to the development of this sector and the achievement of the single european transport area. let them say 'no' to exceptions that block a greener, greener, safer fleet, such as restrictions on vehicles weighing more than six tonnes; let them say 'no' to measures that block the needs of operators to be able to respond to seasonal peaks and let them say 'no' to proposals that fall on workers, because we are, in effect, referring to a directive that explicitly says 'no drivers'. moreover, all vehicles put into circulation in the union must comply with the established emission limits and we must not therefore create european legislative redundancies that say 'no' to the amendment of legislative acts, through this we cannot change them through others and tomorrow we will vote on this report in favour of the transport market.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i now ask you to support the text adopted at the june part-session in the vote tomorrow. in this way we can have a text which will bring legal clarity. it is a market which, as has already been said here, has potential which must be unlocked by us, which must be unlocked here by the european parliament. a flexible rental market which will benefit the cross-border and national freight transport market of the member states and in particular be able to respond to seasonal peaks and to one-off demand. it will bring environmental benefits, because we are talking about a fleet of rental vehicles that is on average four to six years old, is more recent than the fleet of the companies themselves, and therefore brings greater safety to the roads. a proposal which will benefit companies because it will enable them to invest in other areas of development rather than in the fleet itself, which is often underused because of the different rental rules in different countries. we do not need to differentiate between rental and own vehicles, because in the union the regulatory framework is clear and all vehicles in circulation in the union must comply with the emission limits set by european legislation. we must not maintain the limit of more than six tonnes because its application runs counter to the general objectives for the development of the single european transport area. we should allow member states to limit the use of these vehicles for four consecutive months in order to avoid a tax carousel and, of course, to respond to the existing tax differences. finally, we must not create a culture in this parliament of amending legislative acts under discussion by means of other acts. the correct application of this directive will be achieved by including all information in the national electronic registers and by using market information systems.
member of the commission. – madam president, first of all i would like to thank ms mlinar for her second report on gender mainstreaming and the work of the european parliament for this legislature. for the eu and its institutions, gender mainstreaming is not a choice. it is an obligation. it is in the treaty. it is in the acquis. it is in the charter of fundamental rights. gender equality is still not a tangible reality for all. indicators are stagnating. if there is any change towards more equality, it is often quite marginal. this also true unfortunately for eu institutions. the gender balance among the members of the european parliament has hardly changed since 2009, from 35% to 36.4%, and women currently account for 32.1% of european commissioners, or nine out of 28. within the european parliament, the share of women in senior or middle management positions also remains low. in a democracy, gender equality in political office matters. more gender balance in eu institutions can increase citizens’ trust in the eu and improve the functioning of our democracies. indeed, as ms mlinar has stressed, this year political parties and the member states have a chance to show their commitment to gender equality and ensure a balanced representation of women and men in the european parliament and in the top positions of the european institutions. i reiterate my plea for gender balanced nominations. public administrations must be in tune with the people they serve. at the beginning of the mandate of this commission, president juncker set an in-house target of 40% women in middle and senior management by the end of the mandate. by 1 november 2018, women accounted for 37% at senior management level in the commission, while at middle management level, the target of 40% has already been met. the progress comes after sustained efforts to identify, develop and support female talents through targeted initiatives. besides taking stock of the progress during the legislature, the report sets the scene for the next one, and i very much welcome this forward-looking approach. as you know, the commission’s strategic engagement for gender equality has been our framework for promoting gender equality and gender mainstreaming in the eu and beyond. it ends in 2019, together with the term of this commission. so in 2019, we are also taking stock of the progress, identifying gaps and contributing to setting priorities for the future. in this context, the views of parliament are very welcome. the rapporteur. madam president, commissioner, i would like to begin by thanking ernest urtasun and the whole team who worked to make this report a reality. it's been an honor to work with these people in particular. i would also like to thank the shadow rapporteurs from the various parliamentary groups for their contributions and willingness to negotiate. gender inequalities in the european union are a sad reality. the house has already adopted numerous resolutions, which have been discussed and debated on a number of occasions, identifying the various dimensions of inequality between men and women and calling for action to combat it. but let's face it. to combat them, we need to have a gender perspective in all policies and not just statements of intent that change nothing. one of the key areas for this fight is taxation. women are underrepresented in the labour market, with an overall employment rate 12% lower than that of men, and of those women who work, one third do so only part-time. the gender pay gap is 16%, and in pensions it is 40%. years and years of cumulative effect of the various disparities. not only do they earn less, but they are the women who interrupt their careers the most and they are the women who devote the most time to caring for children or family members with disabilities or with permanent support needs. in the european union, 80% of care is provided by informal carers, 75% of whom are women. the direct and indirect taxation policies are also unfavourable to women. the regressive policies we have seen in recent years in taxing labour, companies, consumption or wealth have weakened the redistributive capacity of tax regimes and have resulted in increased income inequality as well as in a structural change in taxation that has shifted the tax burden onto lower income groups, particularly affecting women. with this report, we present specific recommendations to governments to tackle these inequalities in four distinct areas. firstly, in direct taxation, whether in the taxation of personal income, corporation tax or capital and wealth tax. secondly, in indirect taxation, in particular the vat schemes which burden women when basic necessities and hygiene goods continue to be taxed as if they were luxury goods. thirdly, the impact of tax evasion and avoidance that directly affect the resources available to the public services that promote equality par excellence. fourth, and finally, the mainstreaming of gender equality in tax policies, in particular through regular gender impact assessments of tax policies. nothing in this report is impossible to achieve if there is a real political will to ensure that women and men are treated equally. that is the signal that parliament must give tomorrow when it votes on this proposal.

the question that should be asked here is whether or not we have a financial system that can solve the problems of the economy, and the truth is that the same financial system is still a problem for the european union rather than a solution. we remain concerned about the clearing of bank assets without questioning the mechanisms that were at the origin of the speculative spiral that was at the base of the financial crisis. over the course of this year we've had a makeover of the banks' accounts with basel 3 ratios, but the truth is that the leverage levels are still very high. we have had mergers and acquisitions that have created mega banks that will create enormous problems in the future and finally, through the european stability mechanism, we have managed to put governments at the service of the single resolution fund, that is, more public funds for the banks and this naturally reinforces our conviction that only public control of the financial system can put the banks at the service of the economy.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to congratulate my colleague, mr josé manuel fernandes, on the work he has done on the investeu dossier, on the one hand to make it more fair and on the other hand to simplify it by adding other financial instruments which have been scattered until now. investeu is essential for the union, especially at a time when investment lines are still clearly insufficient for europe's development objectives. i therefore believe that to give the european investment bank a decisive role here is to recognise the good work that has been done by this european institution with years of success in this area. there is no doubt that the investeu fund will ensure a share of the union budget dedicated to its main strategic axes, under shared responsibility and in complementarity with other financing instruments of the member states, and this will allow smaller countries, such as portugal, to have more opportunities to see projects come to life that until now, due to unfavourable risk analyses, could not be financed by private investment. ladies and gentlemen, this is the opportunity you have been waiting for for some of the projects that are so essential to combating climate change and decarbonising the economy, to blue growth and sustainable development, and to protecting biodiversity in the oceans and on land.

mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i hope that this european social fund, which is now more than a few hundred years old, will not leave the priorities of the social rights pillar on paper. two conditions must be met: first, it must be a programme that is easy to access and, secondly, it must be demanding in its assignment, satisfying only the real needs of europeans and not contributing to party propaganda. this requires that social and geographical barriers to access to this fund be overcome, while ensuring that only projects that translate into greater social development are supported, notably the youth guarantee programme which aims to support young people in unemployment and lack of training. all this is even more relevant in rural areas with specific contingencies and with fewer economic and employment opportunities, which leads me to my final point: that the european social fund should not forget that we are the europe of the regions and that one of its main objectives is to eliminate the gaps that still exist between them, by providing different responses to different realities.
mr president, the proposal, more than its architecture and objectives, the programmes it involves or the financial distribution it carries out, is compromised by its complete subordination to the european semester, by the insufficient funds, by the conditionality of the programme to the wishes of the commission, as well as by the link it intends to promote with the investeu fund. in order to make the programme more effective, particularly in portugal, we have put forward a number of amendments: subordination of the programme to national development strategies and fully shared management, guaranteeing the value of and increased investment in universal, free and quality public services, particularly in the health and education sectors, the use of the fund as an instrument to tackle social problems and challenges, particularly in creating jobs and integrating poverty-fighting strategies, and a significant increase in the fund's budget. these changes represent an opportunity to give it robustness and scope.

madam president, i voted in favour of this report because the closure of the ignalina nuclear power station in lithuania is special for several reasons. it is special in that it is a pioneering and irreversible dismantling of the two graphite reactor cores, type rbmk, inherited from soviet technology and in every way similar to those used in chernobyl. this is a unique opportunity for the union to acquire knowledge in the field of decommissioning of nuclear installations and radioactive waste management, which justifies the separate investment provided for in the document and in particular also because it is an exception to the polluter pays principle for radioactive waste. why would you do that? because this investment in nuclear energy was imposed on the territory of lithuania by the then soviet union, intended to serve a much larger region, and disproportionate to the size and financial capacity of lithuania. the closure of the nuclear power plant has led to the need to increase energy imports to serve the region and therefore lithuania has been suffering from very high energy costs for years. i therefore understand that the union's co-financing is above the planning of the euratom agreements and i hope that this case can serve as an example for the planned and ongoing dismantling in other member states.
madam president, i voted in favour because i believe that differentiation reflects the idea that europe does not work in a one-size-fits-all approach that has the capacity to adapt to the needs and wishes of european citizens. differentiation must be a constitutional instrument that ensures flexibility without compromising the entire political system and equality between citizens. normalising differentiation is to ensure that we work with the capacities of the member states so that they can pursue the union's development policies without creating a two-speed europe, or europeans of the first and second kind. the process of differentiation must be harmonised, putting an end to the practices of self-exclusion and derogation from the primary law of the union which lead to negative differentiation, distort the homogeneity of european law and undermine the social cohesion of the union. but we cannot afford to give up the union's fundamental values, and so its cornerstones must not be open to differentiation. a future european institutional framework cannot but inevitably include the european pillars of political, economic, social and environmental rights. there is no room for pause in freedom and democracy or in the fight against climate change.

mr president, commissioner, six months after we debated in this house the implementation and monitoring of national strategies to promote the integration of roma communities, we have come to the conclusion that the member states continue to fail to combat discrimination and prejudice against this ethnic minority. the truth is that the reports we receive reveal that roma still live on the margins of society, with limited, if any, access to education, employment, health, housing, which consequently makes them more vulnerable to poverty and social exclusion and reinforces the negative stereotypes associated with the community. ladies and gentlemen, it was in 2011 that the member states committed themselves to adopting national roma integration strategies by 2020, but the truth is that even today we continue to denounce an intention that, in many cases, has never been put into practice. in the face of the challenges of populism and the brutal and cowardly attack on democratic institutions and values in europe, it is now necessary for the european union to respond in a more ambitious and integrated way to the needs of the roma community, which numbers over ten million people, most of whom are citizens of the member states of the european union.

in writing. the deep and protracted crisis of the european union, along with the attack on social achievements and rights and the associated retrogression in the dimension of civilization, also involves a disturbing dimension of attack on democracy itself. the attack on social rights and the attack on democracy go hand in hand. this is how one should read the successive attacks on the national sovereignty of states, the attempt to empty their sovereign institutions and the concentration of power in supranational bodies, at the service of big capital and the great powers. the successive amendments to the rules of procedure of the european parliament, aimed at limiting the involvement of members and smaller political groups, bureaucratising the functioning of parliament and reducing and weakening the instruments of scrutiny of the legislative process and the action of the different eu institutions, must also be read in the light of this more general reality. it is significant that these efforts unite the right and social democracy. we strongly condemn and reject the attempt to create new and serious obstacles to the formation of political groups on the basis of an arbitrary and subjective criterion of 'political affinity'. this is a further step in the process which has been calling into question the democracy and pluralism which the right wing and social democracy hypocritically claim to defend.
mr president, in portugal the ctt, the portuguese post office, has been privatised. the results are disastrous: a sharp increase in fares, 564 stations and stations closed, mail delivery slower than it was 30 years ago, 900 fewer workers. the company has already paid out more than eur 240 million in dividends to shareholders since 2014, with dividends exceeding net income this year. they are now announcing the layoffs of 800 more workers in addition to the 340 that are in the process of being laid off, as well as the sale of more assets and the closure of 22 more stations. the postal services are an indispensable instrument for the social, economic and territorial cohesion of our country. the post office must guarantee a public postal service for the country and its people. the ctt must be freed from ruinous private management and public control of the portuguese post office must be restored. here too, the disastrous liberalization and privatization processes sponsored by the european union must be brought to an end.
mr president, commissioner, on 5 november 2015, the rupture of the mariana dam created a river of mud that, running through 633 kilometres of waterways, reached 39 municipalities, killed 19 people and caused the biggest environmental disaster in brazil. to this day, investigations continue into those responsible without any conviction. three years later, the new dam, the one at the mina do feijão, in the brumadinho complex, owned by the same company that owns mariana, vale, collapsed again and the avalanche of mud and mining waste has already caused 75 deaths, 279 disappearances and left several indigenous tribes without their crops and drinking water. with only 3% of brazil's 24,000 dams inspected and brumadinho identified by the national water agency late last year as one of 45 at risk of failure, the question now is not whether there will be another tragedy, but when it will happen again. in view of the imminence of this threat, the european union cannot but urge the brazilian federal and state authorities to identify those responsible for this disaster and to tighten the licensing rules for new mining dams and to implement more effective and more demanding monitoring systems.

when discussing the deepening of emu and the banking union, the possible merger between deutsche bank and commerzbank is on the agenda today. this merger would create a banking mega-group with some eur 2 billion in assets, with a top position at european level. several analysts recall that the two institutions have had some difficult years. the commission also found that the bank's capital needs were not sufficiently addressed by the restructuring plan. in short, the low level of own funds of both institutions suggests a scenario of some reserve. this merger between these institutions will accentuate the problem of the large financial institutions with systemic effect that were the basis for public intervention after the financial crisis. it will create excessive market power, potentiating abuses of dominant positions with serious damage to the economy. in short, it will confirm once again the role of the ecb and the single supervisory system as active promoters of banking concentration within the european union.

on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, i would like to extend a special greeting to our commissioner karmenu vella and i would also like to congratulate my colleague alain cadec and all those involved in the work carried out during the negotiations. i think we got a good end result. first, because it ensured that one of the most ambitious objectives of the cfp, namely to achieve the maximum sustainable yield by 2020, was maintained. i know that in some cases this is an ambitious objective and that there is still a long way to go, but we have kept this ambition in the regulation. secondly, because we have included improvements in the protection of sensitive species, important resources and habitats, in particular through the possibility of designating protection areas for juveniles and for the recovery of springs. regionalization and proximity of management, advocated in pluriannual plans such as this one, are a solution that has already proven itself. an example of this is the case of the azores horseradish, for which a number of specific management measures have been established as a result of technical measures implemented. the most up-to-date scientific data show that there has been recovery of this resource. the regional plan drawn up in conjunction with the fishermen has produced positive results both in terms of recovery of the species and in terms of the financial benefits obtained. the measures also involved the establishment of marine protected areas and fisheries reserves. this management tool is unavoidable and must be multiplied. finally, these plans will have to contribute to the landing obligations, but we have to allow for exceptions: individuals caught below minimum size and with proven relevant survival rates shall be returned to the sea. bringing fish ashore that are not interested and that, if they survive, can reproduce and later be caught with greater economic benefit is doubly positive. i would therefore like to stress in particular the exception for the fishery for plaice and berries in the western atlantic.

member of the commission. – mr president, i would like to thank all of you for your interventions and valuable views, and also for the broad support for this multiannual plan. i have taken due note of the various points raised during this debate. but let me reiterate, it is of utmost importance that the multiannual plans are made straightaway fit for purpose. to ensure this, they need to provide the tools to ensure a practical and sustainable management framework for our mixed demersal fisheries. the co-legislators have achieved this with the western waters plan. it was not only important to have a multiannual plan in place for the western waters but also to have the multiannual plan as soon as possible. this is the way to implement the regionalisation of the common fisheries policy (cfp), which is an important feature of the latest cfp reform in 2013. it provides a tool to facilitate reaching the maximum sustainable yield (msy) objective by defining ranges of fishing opportunities in line with msy. this provides the necessary flexibility to reduce potential choke problems. with the 2020 deadline approaching, it is important to have this possibility in place as soon as possible. it also allows member states to recommend, via joint recommendations, appropriate technical measures to improve the conservation of the stocks, to facilitate the landing obligations and to protect juveniles.
mr president, commissioner, the objective of the protocol implementing the fisheries agreement between the european union and côte d'ivoire, now renewed for a period of six years, is to provide fishing opportunities for vessels of member states of the european union in the waters of côte d'ivoire, on the basis of the best available scientific advice and in compliance with the recommendations of the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas. it is hoped that this protocol will enable the european union and the republic of côte d'ivoire to work more closely together to promote the responsible exploitation of fisheries resources in côte d'ivoire's waters and to support côte d'ivoire's efforts to develop the local economy on the basis of the contribution made by the fisheries sector. the committee on fisheries has, at my suggestion, in good time considered making a more detailed assessment and consideration of this agreement, its history and its future prospects, and has made some considerations and recommendations which it is now hoped the european commission will take into account during the period of validity of the protocol and in any future renegotiation. the first fisheries agreement between côte d'ivoire and the european community was signed in 1990. although 28 years have passed, almost 29, the results achieved so far in the field of sectoral cooperation are shockingly meagre. this is a reality that needs to change. the agreement should promote the effective and sustainable development of the offshore fisheries sector and related industries and activities by increasing the value added that is left in the country as a result of the exploitation of its natural resources. there is a need for better coordination between sectoral support under the fisheries agreement and the instruments available under development cooperation, including the european development fund, through both their respective national programming and regional programming within the west africa region. the european commission should take the necessary measures, including the possible revision and increase of the sectoral support component of the agreement, along with the improvement of the conditions to increase the absorption rate of this support in order to ensure an effective reversal of the path followed in the last decades. as in other countries in the region, it is considered necessary to improve the quantity and reliability of information on catches and on the conservation status of fishery resources in general, as well as to develop côte d'ivoire's own capacity to obtain such information. i would just like to thank the shadow rapporteurs for their work and attention to these reports and stress that parliament must be immediately and fully informed at all stages of the procedures relating to the protocol or its renewal. we propose, in particular, that the european commission should submit an annual report to parliament on the results of the multiannual sectoral programme and on compliance with the requirement to declare catches.
mr president, this agreement is part of the network of tuna fisheries agreements in west africa, which also includes the agreements with guinea-bissau, cape verde and são tomé and príncipe, among others. it's a deal of great geographical relevance. the port of abidjan is one of the leading ports in west africa, both in terms of landings and in terms of fish processing. its importance, particularly for tuna, demonstrates the relevance of this agreement to the union fisheries, considering that part of this fish is ultimately destined for this market. i would like to conclude by stressing my support for the agreement, stressing the need to ensure that the associated funds are effectively used to develop the socioeconomic sector of the blue economy of côte d'ivoire, but also to control the activity, in particular to ensure that the fish marketed and processed in the port of abidjan does not come from iuu fishing.
member of the commission. – mr president, thank you honourable members. as requested by the rapporteur, the sectoral support will be largely increased to over eur 400 000. that’s almost an increase of some 35% to support local development and improved governance of fisheries. also, the commission will keep the parliament regularly informed of the implementation of this agreement and will also provide reports both on the catches and on the use of the eu financial contributions. the conclusion of the new protocol to the fisheries partnership agreement will reinforce the stable relationship between the eu and côte d’ivoire for mutually beneficial outcomes. this new protocol is fully consistent with the eu’s ocean governance agenda, under which the union cooperates with third countries with the purpose of improving compliance with international measures, including combating illegal, unreported, and unregulated fishing (iuu). to conclude i would like to thank the speakers for their comments, for their support for this protocol and especially the rapporteur, mr ferreira, for his report.
mr president, i would like to thank the various speakers and, in conclusion, to stress one of the points in the resolution we will be voting on tomorrow: the fisheries partnership agreement between the european union and côte d'ivoire, in its implementation and in any revisions and/or renewals, must take into account and be in line with the relevant strategic plans, while fully respecting the sovereignty of côte d'ivoire, aiming at the development of the coastal-marine fisheries sector, in particular by strengthening controls and surveillance of the côte d'ivoire exclusive economic zone and measures to combat illegal, undeclared and unregulated fishing, supporting the construction and/or rehabilitation of fish markets, landing piers and ports, in particular but not only in the port of abidjan, improving working conditions in the sector, including working conditions for women, in particular in the fisheries sector, enabling women to work in the fishing industry, and being more closely linked to the development of a more effective system of scientific and technical support, enhancing the sustainability of the global fisheries system, supporting the conservation of fishery resources, and enhancing the capacity of the marine artisanal fisheries sector. finally, we urge the european commission to provide information on the benefits that the implementation of the protocol will bring to local economies in terms of employment, infrastructure, improvement of social conditions, as well as on catches and the state of conservation of resources, so that we can better assess the impact of the agreement on the marine ecosystem and fishing communities.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, among the reports we are debating here today, i would like to mention in particular the important report on european citizenship which i have been following as the shadow rapporteur of my political group. i should like to begin where it should, by thanking our fellow rapporteur, maite ruiz, for the excellent spirit of cooperation which we have maintained and which has enabled us to produce a report which i believe is a good contribution by this parliament to qualifying democracy and strengthening european citizenship. this report comes at a crucial moment for the european project. at a time when this european project is under violent attack, the elections next may are decisive for the future of this european union that we have built over the last decades. it is therefore now more than ever that europeans must become fully aware of their status as citizens and exercise their first right of citizenship, the right to participate in politics by voting. but this requires an educational campaign to raise awareness of citizens' rights, starting with the right to vote, a major campaign against abstention. and we must also take on and recognise the truly european dimension of these elections and that they take place without obscure interference through social networks. only then will we have a european citizenship that meets the expectations of european citizens and qualifies our democracy.
mr president, the charter of fundamental rights of the european union is the result of an attempt to give a social appearance to an integration project which, by its very nature and its policies, is clearly anti-social. this is another reason why this document is a minimalist and reductive acquis, compared, for example, with the universal declaration of human rights, national constitutions - as is the case with the constitution of the portuguese republic - or even with the social charter of the council of europe. the recent years have laid bare the incompatibility between the action and policies of the european union and the realisation of economic and social rights. the european union has been a blocking factor and not a promoter of rights. we look at the results of the european semester, at economic governance, at the budgetary treaty, at the action of the eurogroup, at the european commission, at the interventions of the troika, all of which are genuine instruments of compressing fundamental rights. it is becoming increasingly clear that the fundamental rights of peoples are and will be asserted against all this panoply of instruments which constrain their sovereign development.
rapporteur. – mr president, i will speak in portuguese, but first i would like to apologise to the chamber, to the president and to the vice-president of the commission, for being late. our way of life involves a lot of flying and when you are flying you cannot control the flight time. i am happy to have been able to attend a significant part of the debate and to speak at the end of it. senhor presidente, queria dizer que é com imenso orgulho que apresento este relatório, desde logo porque ele teve o apoio unânime da comissão de assuntos constitucionais e, portanto, significa que se conseguiu aqui uma base que é uma base, eu diria, muito consensual dentro dos membros da comissão de assuntos constitucionais, e que, portanto, foi de um lado da bancada até ao outro percorrendo todos os grupos políticos que ali estão representados, portanto isto é o primeiro ponto. o segundo é que era extremamente necessário fazer a reforma do estatuto do provedor de justiça europeu porque, talvez nem todos tenham consciência, mas o estatuto que nós tínhamos era anterior ao tratado de lisboa e, portanto, trata-se aqui, efetivamente, de fazer chegar, pelo menos ao eleitorado, nas eleições de 2019, um texto que esteja já em conformidade com o direito da união que já está em vigor há praticamente dez anos. devo, aliás, dizer que também é motivo de grande orgulho que este é um dos casos em que o parlamento tem iniciativa legislativa e, portanto, é um caso singular do ponto de vista constitucional e por isso é um relatório também importante nessa medida. dito isto, eu queria dizer quais foram os dois princípios que conduziram a feitura deste relatório. o primeiro foi: é preciso um novo estatuto adequado, mas não queremos, em caso nenhum, em caso nenhum romper com a ideia de soft law e de soft power que anda associada ao provedor de justiça. o poder do provedor de justiça, o poder do ombudsman é um poder suave, é um poder soft, é um poder informal. no dia em que ele passe a ser parte num processo, no dia em que ele passe a ter intervenções vinculantes, seja ainda que em domínios muito acabados, ele perde o seu poder. uma das razões pelas quais as instituições europeias, os vários corpos da união europeia, confiam no provedor é porque sabem que o seu poder não é vinculante. a sua autoridade e a sua força vêm da sua fraqueza, vêm da sua fragilidade. ora havia algumas propostas e algumas tentativas de tornar numa espécie de procurador o provedor. ora o provedor não é um procurador, o provedor não é um prossecutor, não é um ministério fiscal e, portanto, neste sentido, não é um pubblico ministero, como dizem os italianos e, portanto, é fundamental manter este soft power e creio que esse equilíbrio nós mantivemos. depois a segunda preocupação foi reforçar a independência, a imparcialidade, a isenção do provedor, dando-lhe todos os meios para que ele possa atuar, garantindo que ele pode ter acesso a todos os órgãos e a todas as entidades e pode ter acesso a informação confidencial embora, evidentemente, fique vinculado por essa confidencialidade. mas isso pode ser muito importante porque muitas vezes para chegar a algumas das suas conclusões, nos vários inquéritos que abre, nas várias diligências que faz, a capacidade de, por um lado, aceder a documentos confidenciais ou a informação confidencial, mas manter a confidencialidade dá-lhe, efetivamente, este estatuto. por isso o que nós fizemos foi também reforçar algumas das suas garantias de independência até no seu estatuto financeiro, etc., foi dar aqui alguma, diria eu, tradução prática à garantia de independência, de imparcialidade e de isenção do provedor e, portanto, manter o seu estatuto de soft power. eu dizia que são estas as duas grandes armas de intervenção, depois há pequenos ajustamentos, pequenos acertos, nos direitos de queixa, num conjunto de pequenas matérias, que eu julgo que aperfeiçoam, mas não rompem com aquela que é a tradição de uma instituição, que até agora funcionou bem, e, enfim, costuma-se dizer, até no futebol, que, em equipa que ganha não se mexe. e, portanto, numa equipa como esta, que é uma equipa ganhadora, que é a da provedoria de justiça europeia, evidentemente que nós mexemos o menos possível, mexemos o essencial para reforçar o seu estatuto e estamos muito satisfeitos que se tenha conseguido um grande consenso na comissão de assuntos constitucionais. oxalá amanhã no plenário esse consenso se traduza também numa votação que reforce o provedor de justiça europeu como um órgão no qual, não apenas as entidades europeias, mas especialmente os cidadãos da união europeia, podem confiar e confiar plenamente. é ele o seu defensor, é ele o seu provedor.
mr president, the debate on the future of europe was in fact a debate on the future of a process of capitalist integration which is in conflict with the principles and values of civilization, the result of the struggle of the peoples of the european continent. the european union is not europe. the future of europe is not confined to the scenarios in which the european union has wanted to limit the debate on its own future. it was soon clear that this debate was nothing more than a set-up, with conclusions drawn from the outset, and that what was intended was an exercise in justifying a new leap forward, deepening the neoliberal slope of integration, along with an even greater concentration of power in the major powers, the practical result of the end of the principle of unanimity which they now openly defend. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force. the future of europe cannot pass through here. it will be the struggle of the workers and the peoples that will shape a future more in line with their interests and aspirations.
mr president, artificial intelligence and robotics are helping to increase the social productivity of human labour. increased productivity creates the conditions for real wage increases and a better life, but this potential is constantly called into question in capitalist society in the interests of defence and profit-making. the capitalist appropriation of new technologies, with the replacement of workers by machines, the competition between workers and machines, the pressure to make capital profitable, drives exploitation and job insecurity. but the new technologies, instead of taking away the work of some and intensifying and degrading the work of others, could and can, conversely, be used to reduce significantly, without loss of income, the working time of all workers, reducing work rates, reducing unemployment rather than increasing it. a reduction in working time without reduction in wages or social benefits that would spread the existing workload across all workers. this is a very topical fight.
in writing. with this own-initiative report, the european parliament adopts a set of fundamental principles for the common values of the union to be embedded in the main pillars of the emerging digital society. in particular, it stipulates that the development of artificial intelligence (ai) must be in line with and respect fundamental rights, freedoms, dignity, privacy and be focused on meeting the concrete needs of people. the european union cannot but fight the global battle for innovation and scientific and technological leadership in strategic areas such as ai. to combat this with fewer financial resources and the necessary ethical guarantees in the use of data, it is necessary to adopt a different approach, focusing on the positive transformation of industrial and social organisation models, measuring the impacts of this transformation and exploiting the dynamics of change to increase the competitiveness and intelligence of production systems, combating inequalities and making public service systems such as health, safety or education and training systems sustainable. this view has prevailed in the ongoing negotiation of the digital europe programme in which i represent the s&d group and must prevail in the development of the digital society in the eu.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i was part of the delegation that went to managua from 24 to 27 january and i was able to witness the high price that journalists and students pay for the courage to denounce the brutal repression of the regime of daniel ortega. universities are cutting off the public funding they are entitled to, students are being charged with terrorism for participating in peaceful protests, are being sentenced to heavy prison sentences, and those who manage to escape are having difficulty resuming their education at the public universities of the countries where they have taken refuge. to silence any dissenting voice, the government loots newspaper offices and, on the charge of terrorism, arbitrarily detain journalists and force others into exile. if, during my visit to el chipote, i have verified the shameful and degrading conditions in the dungeons where journalists miguel mora and lúcia pineda are detained, i have received reports that these conditions, contrary to what we were assured, have worsened since our visit. lucia was isolated from the other prisoners and miguel transferred to el infiernillo. i therefore urge the nicaraguan authorities to release these political prisoners immediately or to allow them to await the hearing scheduled for 15 march under house arrest.
mr president, domestic violence, a concrete expression of the more general violence against women in our society, continues to take on brutal and intolerable forms. in fact, she will always be, in any case, brutal and intolerable. beyond the shock and commotion of public knowledge of each new concrete case, action is needed. action is needed to prevent, combat and eradicate. action is needed to provide effective and timely protection for victims, to strengthen social support and access to justice. it is necessary to act to transform the reality underlying domestic violence, to act on the economic, social, political and cultural causes that are at the origin of domestic violence, on the psychological dynamics that interact with them as a result of various dependencies. to act against all forms of daily violence, more or less concealed, against all forms of discrimination against women, in wages, in work, in life, against all obstacles to the full recognition of equality.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the budget is properly managed. mr president, a europe that protects is a necessity. the protection of human life must be our priority. in 2017, in portugal, we had a huge tragedy where more than a hundred people lost their lives. it's something that can't be repeated. this new mechanism proves the solidarity of the european union, it shows that pride alone is not the solution, sharing, and sharing sovereignty in some areas, such as civil protection, reinforces that same sovereignty. there can be no relaxation of the member states, they must continue to invest in protection, and protection is the solution. there is another concern, which was a finding, there are finance ministers who did not want this agreement and who have been haggling over two hundred million euros, a ridiculous sum, for 2019 and 2020, something that we have to emphasize here and also emphasize the commitment of commissioner christos stylianides, of rapporteur gardini, so that we could have an agreement that, in effect, shows that the european union saves lives and is worthwhile.
mr president, today marks another decisive step in the creation of the new european union civil protection mechanism, under which the union will be able to provide better assistance, in a coordinated manner and to complement the action of the member states. the overwhelming disasters that have occurred across europe in recent years have put the current voluntary-contribution-based european civil protection mechanism to the test. let us recognize that in the key moments there was never a lack of solidarity, but what was often lacking were the resources needed for the required response. the creation of the reserve of assets at european level, as envisaged in resceu, is urgent, it is essential, and the report under discussion makes it possible to achieve this. i congratulate the rapporteur. on the basis of the principles of solidarity and responsibility-sharing, the union will be able to better assist its citizens. support to member states to improve risk management and to strengthen existing national measures will also be stepped up. but, commissioner, the implementation of these policies cannot be postponed. the operational capacity of resceu should not be jeopardised by the political and bureaucratic difficulties resulting from the ongoing debate on the financial perspectives 2021-2027. as rapporteur appointed by the european parliament in the context of the post-2020 multiannual financial framework, i recently put a priority written question to the commission on the timetable for the publication of the legislative proposal for the financing of this mechanism. if it were published today, it wouldn't be so soon. it would be intolerable if that publication did not take place in this parliament. catastrophes do not wait. european citizens need resceu now.
mr president, commissioner, it is positive and desirable that cooperation in the field of disaster response should be increased, especially in the case of major disasters. such cooperation can and should involve coordination of efforts, the lending of resources, and also the sharing of certain means of response. the european union has not done all it can in this area. the 2017 fire situation in portugal has clearly demonstrated this. in the field of disaster prevention too, things have fallen short of what was desirable. we are also very concerned about the prospect of further cuts in the funding for economic and social cohesion, which is where much of the resources for disaster prevention come from. disasters are unjust, they almost always strike more states and populations with the least means of defending themselves. but it is important that we do not go from a situation where we do not do everything we can to a situation where we want to do more than we should, and here, just a note to say that it is very important that the chains of command remain at the national level. the usurpation of chains of command to a supranational level, as envisaged in the european commission's initial proposal, would raise serious risks, which fortunately could be avoided by correcting this part of the proposal and keeping the chains of command at the national level, where they should in fact be.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not undermined by the excessive deficit. mr president, this matter requires a serious and not demagogic discussion for the sake of the health of europeans, the work of our farmers and the political seriousness of what we are doing here. it is obvious that we can use pesticides in a more sustainable way, and it is also obvious that farmers have the greatest interest in maintaining a clean and sustainable environment. your livelihood depends on it. i therefore regret the general tone of this document which attributes agriculture as the main cause of the misuse of these products, when we know that their use is widespread among professionals and non-professionals in other sectors as well. climate change is triggering the emergence of new pests that we must urgently combat and thus protect our crops and production. the way forward is not to eradicate pesticides, but to focus on the greatest number of available low-risk active substances and on the selective administration of these products. i would like to say again that the european union has the safest agri-food system in the world and that in the most recent studies 97.2% of samples of agricultural products show residues well below the legal limit. on the contrary, recent research has shown that pesticide residues are more prevalent in products of organic origin than in conventional production. these are the facts.
mr president, the unsustainable use of pesticides is causing serious environmental, public health and food safety problems. this unsustainable use is inseparable from the current production models favoured by the common agricultural policy and the trade policies of the european union. productivist, export-oriented models that leave food production, something of obvious strategic importance in any society, in the hands of agribusiness. the same policies that have been and continue to be devastating for small and mediumsized farms and family farms are of crucial importance for the sustainable occupation of the territory and the food sovereignty of the states. there is no doubt that stricter procedures are needed to control the use of pesticides. there is no doubt that new products and processes need to be developed which involve fewer risks and less impact on health and the environment. but it is also necessary to make a profound change in the existing production models and the policies that make them possible.
madam president, commissioner, feeding a world population of over seven billion people presents challenges to which the agri-food industry has responded with high efficiency. however, we cannot let it all go to waste in the production game. on the one hand, we are restricting or reducing biodiversity and agro-diversity through the use of pesticides of high toxicity and with an unknown impact on human health. on the other hand, we continue to approve genetically modified organisms that are resistant to the pesticides we select for commercial convenience. it is not surprising, therefore, that there is a low availability of low-risk plant protection products, including biological ones, of which there are only 13 out of a total of 500 substances available on the market. the reality is that the insufficient implementation of the directive and the discussions of approval by member states create unequal conditions of competition in europe. there is a barrier to the full market uptake of sustainable alternatives with a consequent reduced attractiveness for farmers who obviously prefer the most cost-effective options in the short term.
madam president, i would first of all like to thank ivo belet for his excellent work. europe is made of solidarity and cooperation. unfortunately, health captured in the principle of subsidiarity is witness to the greatest asymmetries within the european union. the hope that this directive on crossborder healthcare represented does not seem to have reached its full intention. patients don't know it exists, healthcare professionals don't know it or use it, and payers raise the biggest bureaucratic and administrative barriers for them. but we cannot just throw this directive on the shelf of the intention books. we must make the directive work. it is crucial for patients and for member states, especially for the people who benefit most from the european scale, which are the citizens with rare diseases, including some of the cancer diseases. cooperation through european reference networks must be stimulated to attract knowledge and must evolve towards becoming genuine centres of expertise within the union. i also want to mention the importance of ehealth as it can be the bridge needed to connect the far-flung margins of state bureaucracies, but there is one thing ehealth cannot do for us and that is to unite us on the different policies regarding neonatal screening and vaccination programmes. this is another step we still have to take towards a fairer europe.
madam president, commissioner, since 2008 the european parliament has defined that the challenge of water scarcity and droughts in the european union must have a systemic approach to water management, which includes less consumption of drinking water, especially for purposes where it is not needed. more recently, through the circular economy action plan, we have returned to this point, but it is urgent that the commission follow up with a plan 2.0 to keep water resources as one of the priority areas of intervention. the reuse of properly treated reclaimed water for irrigation purposes must be conditional on respect for the environment and human and animal health. it is therefore clear that reusing water cannot lead to eutrophication of soils and ground and surface water, which would be disruptive to ecosystems and contribute to the reduction of biodiversity. the role of the competent authorities in cooperation with end-users in promoting water reuse is also important. finally, there is an urgent need to renew the water distribution infrastructure, which is old and in poor condition, resulting in significant losses and other losses on a daily basis as well as in the financial resources invested.
madam president, i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe that more must be done by the european union in terms of coordinating the response capacity to the civil and humanitarian crises that the various member states may face in the coming years. while acknowledging the recent efforts made by this mechanism in portugal, i would stress that we are at a stage of negotiation which is crucial to its full development and implementation and for which greater speed and less institutional deadlock between member states are required. that is why i believe it is time to recognise that the european civil protection mechanism, by promoting a stronger collective european response, plays a crucial role in shaping a common strategy capable of addressing rapidly the climate and humanitarian challenges we face today. i reiterate the need to do more and better, starting with the consolidation of resceu as a reserve capacity to complement the resources that each member state already has at its disposal.

author. mr president, commissioner, allow me first of all to highlight the work and cooperation with all the colleagues who have contributed to the construction of this question and resolution. the evidence of the backsliding of women's rights in the european union is not one to be missed. in the last decade women's labour, economic, social and cultural rights have been severely compromised. the right-wing policies, sponsored or imposed by the european union, have led to an increase in the precariousness of industrial relations. the policies of low wages, the deregulation of working hours, the increase in the retirement age, which impose multiple discriminations on women. at the same time, social and public service responses have deteriorated. the degradation or absence of public services to ensure assistance and care, whether in childhood, in sickness or old age, constitutes a shameful lack of responsibility on the part of the states in responding to the needs of the population, contributing to situations that exclude women from the labour market. the protection of maternity, as defined by law, is undermined by the enormous pressure on women workers to renounce their rights, a direct consequence of the precariousness of working relationships. the degradation of public health services and the assault on the right to sexual and reproductive health is another element of the attack on women's rights. attempts to reverse or prevent the granting of rights in the voluntary termination of pregnancy and to ensure that women are provided with adequate reproductive health care are known and unacceptable. global levels of poverty and unemployment, which particularly affect women, remain at unsustainable levels. the gender pay and pension gaps remain unacceptably high. the increase in violence against women, which has been taking place during this same period, cannot be separated from the curtailment of rights and the deterioration of the socioeconomic conditions of the population. in my country, since the beginning of the year, nine women and one child have been killed as victims of domestic violence. these figures are a brutal and growing expression in the european union of the multiple forms of violence against women, whether domestic violence, sexual violence and harassment, violence and harassment in the workplace, violence and exploitation in prostitution. the enshrinement of equality and the fight against violence in law, whether in the national or international sphere, as exemplified by the istanbul convention, are important and necessary steps, but enshrinement in law is not enough. there can be no room for make-believe policies in equality policies or quick fixes in the fight against all forms of violence. the successful prevention, combating and eradication of violence against women in its many forms is inseparable from the realisation of women's rights in law and in life. specific programmes for the protection and support of victims, measures to strengthen social support, access to justice, shelter homes, mental health care in the areas of prevention, treatment and rehabilitation, responses that guarantee economic autonomy and that guarantee, in practice, confidence and security that the law does indeed protect women victims of violence at the various stages until they rebuild their lives, need to be set up. the path of women's economic and social emancipation is crucial in the construction of equality and the fight against all forms of violence. this requires placing work at the centre of the solution to the inequalities between men and women. equality in life, which requires the fight against unemployment and precarious employment, the enhancement of wages and the elimination of wage discrimination, the equal participation of women in all sectors of activity, the right to be a mother and a worker with rights, social protection of maternity and paternity in the personal areas of health and social security, the creation of a public network of child, youth, elderly, disabled and victims of violence support facilities, access to the national health service and protection of sexual and reproductive health, the protection of women victims of prostitution and trafficking for the purpose of sexual exploitation and sexual harassment or in the workplace. the fight for equality and against all forms of violence is a fight for all, a fight which requires states to assume their responsibilities and to assert themselves as an indispensable element in the promotion of equality and the fight against all forms of violence.
president-in-office of the council. – madam president, i would like to thank the european parliament for the opportunity to discuss such an important subject here today. i will focus particularly on the rights of intersex people, which should be protected in all member states and which are the subject of one of your questions to the council. i would like to begin by stating very clearly that violence and discrimination have no place in european society. we should not forget that romania’s presidency of the council of the european union has as one of its priorities combating discrimination of any kind. the principles and values enshrined in the eu treaties, the charter of fundamental rights of the european union and eu legislation are not up for negotiation or bargaining. in europe, the commitment to decency, tolerance and equal treatment is the very foundation of the idea of a just society. this is a source of pride for europeans, and for good reason: our society is becoming more inclusive and diverse than ever before. together, we have taken steps to guarantee equal treatment for women, ethnic minorities, sexual minorities, persons with disabilities, religious minorities and citizens of all ages. and we continue to work on that. i believe that all three eu institutions fundamentally agree on the direction of the journey – towards even greater emancipation and tolerance. i am proud to say that the council has already adopted legislation that clearly prohibits discrimination on the grounds listed in article 19 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union, but, as you well know, the anti-discrimination law book remains incomplete. we have better protection, at eu level in respect of discrimination on some grounds rather than others. as many of you have often reminded us, the council has not yet exhausted its legal capabilities in that regard. i would like now to turn to the specific issue of violence and discrimination against intersex people. for, indeed, you speak of real suffering in the questions you have put to the council and the commission today. i am proud to point out that, in june 2016, the council responded positively to the commission’s list of actions to advance lgbti equality. it was the first time that the council had adopted a declaration devoted to the advancement of equality for lesbian, gay, bisexual, transgender and intersex persons. so it was a historic moment. in its conclusions the council invited the commission to promote the measures outlined in this list of actions and to report on a regular basis on the progress achieved. the council also invited the member states to take their own actions to combat discrimination on the grounds of sexual orientation and gender identity. it is, of course, the responsibility of the member states to hear that call. honourable members, the advancement of equal treatment is a joint effort to which we all must contribute. when it comes to cooperation at eu level, the commission’s list of actions, including the exchange of good practice, is to be welcomed. the commission has also undertaken a campaign of awareness raising. the fundamental rights agency is also making important contributions by mapping out the situation of intersex people, including the problems they face in terms of birth registration as well as the matter of non-necessary surgery – which you mentioned – and the closely related problem of informed consent. this is, first and foremost, a question of medical ethics, for which the member states are responsible and accountable. the issue of birth registration is also a national matter. indeed, when it comes to the more specific issues identified in your question, it is the member states that have the key competences. however, this does not mean there is no european dimension or solution. of course there is. as you know, the category of intersex is not included on the list of prohibited discrimination grounds in the treaties, but i believe it is obvious that extending the right to equal treatment for intersex people is entirely within the spirit of our common european values and our common campaign for inclusiveness. as we pursue, improve and defend our values, we all benefit from constructive exchanges, including the one we are holding here today and the vote you will have tomorrow. thank you for your attention. i would like to remind you, as i said at the beginning, that the romanian presidency of the council has as one of its priorities the fight against discrimination, and this is one of the opportunities that we are proud to have.
member of the commission. – madam president, let me first start by thanking parliament for tabling this debate on the discrimination against lgbti people. the commission is committed to the advancement of equality of lgbti people and welcomes that parliament has ensured, through the report, that these issues, and particularly the situation of intersex persons, are kept high on the political radar screen. it is exactly for this reason that we published a list of actions to advance lgbti equality, in december 2015. in the coming months we will present a third report on the implementation of the list of actions. i will come back to this list of actions at the end of this opening remark. let me say a few words on the problems related to intersex people who, as we all know and as you mentioned many times, are having serious problems because they do not fit into the main gender categories – female and male. the unnecessary surgical operations and treatments that intersex children may undergo are especially worrying since they are not able to give their fully informed consent due to their young age. however, according to the treaty on the functioning of the european union, the definition of health policies and the organisation and delivery of health services, fall under the responsibility of member states. the commission respects the principle of subsidiarity in the area of ethical accountability for the surgical treatment that intersex young people and children undergo. the possibility to provide a third gender option at birth or in public documents are also competences of the member states. regarding transgender people, member states have to ensure that the relevant laws are applied in accordance with the latest international human rights standards. the european court of human rights ruled that the condition of compulsory sterilising surgery or treatment for legal gender recognition violated article 8 of the convention. so i will now come back to our list of actions which aim to advance lgbti rights. within the framework, or based on this list of actions, the commission has carried out several activities to raise awareness of the difficulties that transgender and intersex people experience. it recently published a new report on trans and intersex equality rights in europe. we also financially support civil society organisations whose work also includes promoting transgender and intersex equality. we have released awareness raising videos and we organised a good practice exchange between the member states on these topics. upon the request of parliament, the pilot project health4lgbti was carried out, which also focused on the specific health issues transgender and intersex people face. as part of the list of actions, the commission also monitors and enforces existing rights of lgbti people under the free movement directive. the commission welcomes the recent ruling by the court in the coman case and the commission will monitor member states’ compliance with the eu free movement acquis as regards issues related to sexual orientation. the commission will continue to implement the list of actions to advance lgbti rights in 2019. this year a new eurobarometer on discrimination, including relevant questions for lgbti equality, and the second fundamental rights agency lgbti survey will take place. these surveys will provide us with important data that will help us to target our future actions. this year is the year of evaluation and preparatory works for the future, updated lgbti list of actions which should be adopted by the newly established commission and endorsed by parliament. the commission is committed to successfully and positively handing over its work to advance lgbti equality to the next commission, which will have to decide how it will continue these policies. but i will strongly recommend that my successor involve the european parliament and civil society organisations in the design of its future lgbti list of actions, as also suggested in the motion we are debating today. i want also to react to the debate we have heard up to now. the commission continues to promote the adoption of the horizontal equal treatment directive. as you rightly pointed out, i will call it a shameful situation that it has been blocked for years in the council. you are aware that this piece of legislation requires unanimous adoption. that’s why we need to have all the member states on board. when i indicated that after so many years of having this blocked, the treaty offers the option of enhanced cooperation, you will not be surprised that the member states – at least in the reactions i got in the council meetings, but also in many bilateral meetings where i raised this issue – confirmed that they need more time and they do not want us to go for the enhanced cooperation, because this is about fundamental rights. so i just convey this information to you and i will continue to keep pushing because we regularly discuss the prospects of this directive, which i would like to see in a positive way. my last comment: you also raised the issue of hate speech online and hate speech in general. the commission is continuing very intensive work, together with the it sector, to achieve a radical decrease in hate speech online and a decrease in hate speech from the social networks. we are reasonably successful. the it sector is doing what it committed itself to do in the code of conduct and we will continue to do this exercise in the future until the end of my mandate.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, commissioner, after the me too movement thousands of lgbti people shared on twitter stories of discrimination and homophobic bullying under the hashtag mequeer, many of them our fellow europeans. in the 21st century, the european union still lacks comprehensive protection against discrimination on grounds of gender identity, sexual orientation and sexual characteristics. lgbti persons continue to be excluded from genuine freedom of movement and from the union's social inclusion strategies, such as the european pillar of social rights, and there are still asymmetries in the protection of their civil rights and access to basic public services such as education, health or social security in different member states. against this background, commissioner, the commission must develop an inclusive strategy for lgbti people, including the promotion of the civil recognition of intersex people, the implementation of projects to prevent homophobic and transphobic violence in the education systems of the member states and also guaranteeing the necessary protection for lgbti asylum seekers.
