on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, on behalf of the s&d group, we would like to propose that the balas report should be discussed as the first item on wednesday, because we consider that this concern is very important. it is to ensure access to social protection to all european citizens in a situation of mobility across european space. this is an area where european rules are outdated. the balas report has undertaken a very important updating of these rules. this is very good progress from the european parliament as a whole. it is true that the council has not yet been able to reach a united position, but if we discuss and vote as first reading, this parliament can secure the progress we got in this area. this is very important for european citizens. that’s why we propose the item as the first one to be addressed on wednesday.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are protected. one billion euros, madam president, i repeat, one billion euros, is the amount of tax fraud and tax evasion. the equivalent of six times the budget of the european union. the equivalent of the sum of the budgets for health and education in the portuguese case, and these numbers are in the public domain thanks to whistleblowers who are now being persecuted, who are facing the possibility of extradition, like julian assange, or who have faced death themselves, like journalist daphne galizia in malta. as rapporteur for this directive, i consider it to be a very important step forward, but it is still insufficient in this respect. i will continue to work to ensure that whistleblowers are kept completely anonymous and are fully protected, because, in fact, to date, only whistleblowers have been convicted, which is a good indication of the seriousness of the situation, and the fact that multinationals are finally left with impunity which must be reversed, and this directive is indeed a step in that direction.
madam president, madam commissioner, do we all agree with the principle that those who act in the common interest deserve protection? if so, it is because we agree that the defence of the general interest of the community, the common interest, calls for everyone without exception. but if that's the case, why can't we all agree that whistleblowers, if they're acting for the benefit of everyone, deserve protection? no one questions the fact that a worker can report a violation of labour rights to the labour protection authority. what then distinguishes this worker from another who discovers, for example, that his company is evading taxes? obviously, not everything is worth it in the name of the common interest. the application of this principle requires a serious and balanced approach, which reconciles the various apparently conflicting principles. the directive we are discussing today is balanced and reasonable and is aimed primarily at those who, in the context of an employment relationship, encounter illegalities. the text strikes a delicate balance between the duty to report and the protection of state interests, not interfering with national internal security, between the creation of internal channels for the protection of whistleblowers and the financial impact on small and medium-sized enterprises, between freedom of expression and the need for confidentiality. madam president, this is a reasonable text, which deserves our support and which defends the common interest.
madam president, this directive is about transparency and integrity in the european union, because whistleblowers are essential to dismantle the crime that infiltrates companies and captures administrations, governments and even law enforcement agencies. it complements article 38 of the fourth anti-money laundering and terrorist financing directive, which already obliges member states to create protection mechanisms, even for whistleblowers without a direct employment relationship with the instrumentalised organisations. whistleblowers must be given special protection in the committees of inquiry of the european parliament. we have seen the murder of daphne caruana galizia, of jan kuciak, we have seen how antoine deltour was convicted for revealing the luxleaks scandal, we have seen the judicial persecution that almost destroyed rudolf elmer in switzerland and, in my country, portugal, we have today arrested the young rui pinto, the main source of football leaks, because a mafia fund based in malta, clubs, lawyers and obscure interests seem to be able to move justice more than the obligation to go after criminals and confiscate the proceeds of crime. when will the public prosecutor's office and the tax authorities ask rui pinto to cooperate in defending the public interest and recovering assets in portugal, as other european countries have already done? rui pinto should be protected as a whistleblower. thank you, madam commissioner, thank you to virginie rozière and to all the colleagues who have worked on this directive.

madam president, this new banking regulation package is very important. the aim is to strengthen the european financial system so that it is resilient to shocks, trustworthy and no longer a permanent threat to depositors and taxpayers. european rules on bank recovery and resolution are now meeting the highest international standards and banks, especially the largest ones, will have to increase their capital by strengthening their capacity to bear losses. these rules will only come into force after a transitional period, which will allow banks to adapt to the new requirements without affecting their ability to finance the real economy. i would like to thank the rapporteurs gunnar hökmark and peter simon for the good work and cooperation we have had in these risk reduction measures in the banking sector. it is now time to move forward with risk sharing, without which there will be no banking union worthy of the name.
madam president, it's been over a decade since the financial crisis of 2008. four billion euros were spent from public funds to recapitalise the banks. in portugal it was 16 billion euros, ten percent of gdp. after 11 years of crisis, after dozens of regulations, of directives, the question is: what concrete progress has been made? because in portugal we are puzzled why the tap is still open. eur 2 billion was spent on the new bank in 2018 and 2019, and in the current government stability programme a further eur 2,15 billion is planned for 2020 and 2021. let us be clear, these directives, these regulations are just cosmetics. what is really required is public control over the financial system to put the banks at the service of the economy and not the economy at the service of the banks, as is clearly the case.

in writing. the debate on the conclusions of the european council of 10 april on the united kingdom's withdrawal from the eu was marked by a deep sense of sadness following the fire that destroyed notre dame cathedral in paris the day before. the european council's commitment to its reconstruction as a shared task for all eu member states shows the political, economic, social and cultural strength of the partnership. a partnership that the united kingdom has shown intent to leave after a referendum. the eu has robustly defined an exit framework with an agreement that the uk institutions have so far failed to approve or propose an alternative. the extension of the exit time to 31 october was another manifestation of the eu's goodwill. if you accept the deal by may 22, the uk will leave the eu. if it does not, then the uk should hold european elections on schedule and its meps should retain full office until the time of eventual withdrawal. the eu cannot be left out of the british loop. the rules are set, the british have to decide.
member of the commission. – madam president, on behalf of the commission, i would like first of all to express my personal condolences to the families and friends of the hundreds of victims in mozambique, malawi and zimbabwe. definitely our thoughts are with the millions of people affected by this devastating disaster. i have said it many times in this room: europe is about solidarity. europe is about solidarity not only inside europe, but also outside europe. so we stood by the side of all three countries, with emergency funding, with in—kind assistance, and with experts on the ground. allow me to summarise our response, our european response, to this catastrophe. first, funding. just a few hours after the landfall of the cyclone, we released an immediate package of assistance of eur 3.5 million, to support access with air services to cut-off areas and hard-to-reach communities and for immediate shelter, water and sanitation. on 9 april, we mobilised an additional eur 12 million in humanitarian assistance for the three countries to provide shelter, water and sanitation, food, health and psychosocial support. this brings the total eu humanitarian assistance to over eur 15 million. second, in—kind assistance. with separate funding of eur 4 million we helped to deploy through our excellent instrument – the eu civil protection mechanism (ucpm) – relief items and in—kind assistance offered by nine eu member states: portugal, spain, france, austria, uk, luxembourg, germany, italy, and denmark. i am really grateful to these countries. in parallel, we produced and shared 57 copernicus satellite maps upon the request of mozambique, zimbabwe and malawi. third point, on expert teams. we immediately deployed eight eu humanitarian, epidemiology and hydrology experts to the ground, to conduct assessments and support the response in the three countries, including to the recent cholera outbreak, unfortunately. in addition, a civil protection team of 13 people was deployed on 23 march via the eu civil protection mechanism, and of course we also helped to deploy expert teams from our member states. all of them are still in mozambique as we speak and are helping to save lives on the ground. allow me to make a nominal reference to these teams because i strongly believe we have to show to our people that our teams can do excellent work on the ground. as i said, they are still in mozambique because they are real heroes on the ground. portugal sent one search and rescue and one medical team. luxembourg sent a satellite communication team to help with connectivity in affected areas. germany and denmark sent a team to help with water purification – very important especially because of the cholera outbreak. italy, spain and germany sent emergency medical teams and equipment, also very important because of the problem of cholera and maybe other diseases. so it is thanks to resceu, our new system to fight against natural disasters, that we can help decisively the member states to deploy assistance inside and outside the european union. and of course help save more lives as our campaign inside europe. since day one, my team and i are monitoring the situation in the three affected countries around the clock, 24/7. through our emergency response coordination centre (ercc) – you will know about this – in brussels and of course through our experts on the ground. we will continue to lead the international support efforts to the three countries, and you can rest assured that the european union will do its best, our best, to support the longer-term reconstruction needs in the three affected countries.
president-in-office of the council. – madam president, thank you for giving me the time to present our points. i would like to thank the honourable members for inviting me to address this important topic today. disasters call for solidarity, for acting together in support of those in need. disasters teach us valuable lessons. we should translate them into life lessons of strength and resilience. the tropical cyclone idai and the subsequent flooding we witnessed in recent weeks have brought enormous destruction and human losses to southern africa. nearly three million people have been affected in mozambique, malawi and zimbabwe, and almost 1 000 people have lost their lives. many are still missing. in mozambique alone, 240 000 houses have been destroyed, damaged or flooded, more than 160 000 people have been internally displaced, and over 4 000 cholera cases have been have been reported. malaria is on the rise. in malawi, the cyclone and the floods have destroyed many fields of recently planted crops. the potential loss of harvest will have a severe impact on the livelihoods of small—scale farmers who depend on agricultural production. women and children are heavily affected. in zimbabwe, the floods aggravated an existing severe humanitarian crisis caused by drought and a poor harvest. food insecurity and malnutrition, which were already at alarming levels, risk worsening further. in all three countries, the humanitarian needs are enormous. they include food, drinking water, shelter, health and nutrition, as well as education, protection and logistics. the mozambique humanitarian response plan and the zimbabwe flash appeal are asking for a total of usd 342 million. currently only 23% has been funded so more efforts will be needed. as you know, the eu and the member states mobilised quickly in response to these natural disasters. humanitarian and civil protection assistance has been offered by the commission and the member states in support of the affected countries. in support of the eu response to this disaster, the presidency of the council took the necessary actions to raise awareness on the humanitarian and funding needs by opening a monitoring page on the eu’s integrated political crisis response arrangements (ipcr) web platform. in this respect, we moved the ipcr into monitoring mode. this has facilitated a constant exchange of information between the member states, the commission, the european external action service (eeas) and the relevant eu agency on the evolving situation, the humanitarian needs and the eu response. we included the disaster caused by the tropical cyclone idai on the agenda of the relevant council working parties to facilitate in—depth discussions among member states and the commission. we welcome the fact that parliament’s committee on development (deve) is also interested in discussing the situation. our intensive exchanges through various channels have shown that the eu and its member states were quick to react in response to this disaster. member states have contributed extensively by providing humanitarian – and that means more than eur 60 million – and civil protection assistance in kind, including shelters, food and power generators, as well as the deployment of water purification, satellite communication, medical emergency teams, flood rescue and relief modules, experts all provided through the eu civil protection mechanism. a european civil protection team led by a romanian expert has been deployed to mozambique since 23 march 2019 in order to assess the needs and ensure coordination and distribution of the aid provided by member states. there is very good cooperation and coordination on the ground. a number of member states also made available military assets, such as ships, helicopters and aircraft for logistics and transport. member states have allocated substantial amounts of funding to the humanitarian response. thus, the necessary services are currently being contracted and implemented. we welcome the fact that the commission has also allocated more than eur 50 million euros for humanitarian support. this brings us to the importance of implementation. our close consultations with member states and the commission have shown that protection must be at the heart of our emergency response. many families have been separated. women and children are disproportionately affected by displacement, and women and girls are particularly vulnerable to gender—based violence and sexual exploitation. we also need to ensure that education can resume as soon as possible for thousands of children affected by the crisis. lastly, we should consider using cash assistance wherever the circumstances allow for it. this is our analysis of the immediate needs and the emergency response. a post-disaster needs assessment will indicate the necessary action to address the long-term recovery needs. i now look forward to hearing your views and the information that the commission will provide.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, my first word goes, of course, to the victims of this disaster caused by cyclone idai in mozambique, malawi and zimbabwe, and, in making this statement, to the people who were already living very badly, in great poverty and suffering, of course that this is a tragedy which has consequences, i would say, very, very serious for all these families, for all these people. i would like to make three points: firstly, contrary to what has been said here, the european union's reaction was delayed. the european union has been slow to react and this is unacceptable. i would not do so with other countries, but as we are talking about africa and underdeveloped africa, the european union reacted slowly at first. you should have done more, you offered little at first, now you're responding well, but you failed in your initial response. secondly, i would like to say that the psd proposal, which has in fact scheduled this debate for the european civil protection mechanism, shows that it was necessary, that we have to strengthen it further and that it is now working well. finally, i hope that this is not just an episode and that in the recovery the european union will be able to give a response with more readiness and more consistency than it did in the early days, in the early days, when it was weak, it was fragile and it was timid, and if it were not for the portuguese, it would have been even slower.
the aid that the european union has mobilised to respond to the catastrophe caused by cyclone idai and the subsequent floods in mozambique, zimbabwe and malawi is a drop in the ocean of needs facing these countries and their populations. the needs in health care, food, housing, communications, transport, logistics and various infrastructures, civil protection, among others, are immense. in the face of these, the 15 million euros and the team of eleven, just eleven, experts mobilised by the european union fall far short of what is possible and necessary. they are a drop in the ocean of possibilities for the european union which fall far short of what several countries in the international community have already mobilized. in addition to emergency aid, which must be increased without delay, there is also the need to think about the subsequent phases, the necessary reinforcement and possible reprogramming of the european development fund, various sectoral support measures and measures such as the more than fair cancellation of the debt.
madam president, first of all, a word to the families of the victims, a word of friendship also to all the affected populations and above all to the mozambican people. we have timely strengthened the new civil protection mechanism, but it is clear that further strengthening is still needed. the european union is solidary internally and externally, but it is a solidarity which must always be strengthened, especially because climate change and its effects will be increasingly severe. and at the moment, despite the aid that has been given, we need more aid, more resources on the ground. there are over a million children who are in a dramatic and emergency situation, which means that we need to act quickly. and after all this, it is still necessary not to forget the reconstruction. the european union must also take part in this and must increase the amounts for the affected territories, and in particular for mozambique, by means of development aid from the european development fund, for example.
madam president, tropical cyclone idai first impacted mozambique on the night of 14 march 2019, with torrential rains and strong winds, then heading towards zimbabwe and causing massive flooding in malawi. the cyclone left a trail of devastation in its wake. in addition to thousands of displaced people, it destroyed infrastructure and left several regions of mozambique completely vulnerable and dependent on international aid. more than a thousand people were killed in the three countries, 60% of them in mozambique, and many thousands were injured. cholera problems have now begun to cause new casualties, mainly in mozambique. there's an unknown number missing. the european union, as the commissioner said, has already provided financial aid of eur 15 million. at the request of mozambique, the european union civil protection mechanism was also activated. the tenders received came mainly from portugal, but also from austria, germany, denmark, spain, france, italy, luxembourg and the united kingdom. we must continue to help the people affected and bear the consequences of what has happened for the future. this is very important, to take consequences for the future. international cooperation in disaster response and prevention is essential. climate change is a reality that doesn't measure gdp, geography, level of development, wealth or attitude. they are a cross-cutting scourge which must be tackled with effective measures to enable the paris agreement to be implemented and to preserve what is still possible of our planet. my sincere condolences and my full association with the mozambican families of malawi and zimbabwe.
madam president, commissioner, first of all a word to the victims of this cyclone idai. a month after cyclone idai devastated parts of mozambique, malawi and zimbabwe, killing hundreds, thousands of people whose homes and crops were destroyed remain in crowded idp camps, without access to essential water, sanitation and health services and now subject to cholera, typhoid and malaria epidemics. among the affected population are 1.6 million children who, in addition to the threat of these outbreaks, are now also vulnerable to exploitation and labour and sexual abuse. the european union, which has not only activated its civil protection mechanism, albeit belatedly, but has also made available more than 15 million euros in humanitarian aid to respond to this catastrophe, must now also have the political will to make this solidarity compatible with the implementation of the much-talked-about marshall plan for africa, which also focuses on an ecological transition model that makes it possible to make the violence of these extreme natural phenomena the exception, not the rule. european solidarity must be more rapid and more comprehensive. it is not enough to create mechanisms which then do not serve the purposes for which they were created.

president-in-office of the council. – madam president, i would like to thank the honourable members for this discussion on this urgent topic. the eu and its member states are at the forefront of this international emergency response. the recovery and reconstruction phase will last far beyond our presidency. when our foreign minister spoke to the committee on development in january, he explained that disaster risk reduction and the nexus between humanitarian and development assistance are the two key priorities of the romanian presidency in the field of humanitarian aid. the scale of the disaster we are witnessing in southern africa is a painful reminder that there is much work to be done in both of these fields. we need to ensure that our emergency response is complemented by our development cooperation efforts in southern africa. when the emergency phase is over we will need to help the affected countries build back better and improve their resilience and preparedness for future shocks. as much as we would wish otherwise, the scale of climate—related hazard is only set to increase in the future. let us all work together to ensure that the damage and loss of lives this could cause may nevertheless get smaller and smaller.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the european union, which for too long has chosen the worst of options for dealing with the problem of international cyber threats by pretending that it does not exist, cannot now, after scandals such as cambridge analytica, ignore the impact of facebook and google algorithms on european democracies. nor is it the formation of public opinion, especially among young people, through the use of applications that automate and massify hate speech, strengthening eurosceptics and populists who want to paralyse the union from its centre. the risk of the upcoming european elections being manipulated by fake news, disinformation campaigns and cyber attacks is further amplified by the nefarious influence of countries outside the union, as shown by the russian funding of the live.eu campaign which led to the disaster of brexit, as our colleague has just mentioned. the proven interference of mr. putin in the 2017 french elections, or the suspicions of chinese espionage on fifth-generation mobile devices, just to name a few. since these cyber threats know no borders, only a joint eu approach, including information sharing among member states, will prevent the chinese cyber troops or the kremlin's hackers and trolls factories from undermining the credibility of europe's liberal democracies next may.

mr president, commissioner carlos moedas, as desirable as it is, we have seen that innovation does not always translate into proportional benefits. although technological leaps are based on innovation, it still takes time for them to be perfected and for all their potential risks to be assessed and mitigated. we cannot therefore accept that in the areas financed through horizon europe the principle of innovation should override the principle of precaution, not least because i believe that not everything is valid in the field of innovation, especially, commissioner, in areas where we need a paradigm shift, such as tobacco, the use of fossil fuels and certain chemicals in industrial and agricultural areas. we cannot protect by the principle of innovation what is no longer politically defensible. but this caution should not be seen as holding back innovation, because wherever the public interest is at stake, as in the case of health, there are already mechanisms in place to speed up market access for these innovative products. the precautionary principle is to be maintained, firstly because it is based on scientific reservations and a serious assessment of the risk/benefit and cost/benefit ratios, and secondly because it is also a source of impetus for research and development to find solutions that are proven safe and do not harm health or the environment.
mr president, this parliament is right to approve the programmes establishing and implementing horizon europe in conjunction with digital europe, which will also be voted on at this part-session. and a lot of it will be acquired. first, convergence, which combines competitiveness and cohesion and defines that the progress of each country or territory benefits the european union as a whole. second, inclusion and reducing asymmetries, which strengthens our internal capacity and projects our voice outward. thirdly, the strategic autonomy in networks which allows the union's global assertiveness potential to be derived from multiple capacities and competences developed in a decentralised manner. fourthly, the commitment of all projects to the code of ethics for the protection of values and rights shared in the european union. fifthly, the contribution of projects to combating climate change. and finally, perhaps most importantly, putting people at the centre of all research and innovation strategies. i congratulate the rapporteurs on all this. it is very important to vote on the programmes under consideration.

on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, commissioner avramopoulos, ladies and gentlemen, the adoption of this new and strengthened european border and coast guard is a historic step towards a truly shared protection of our common external borders. it is a historic step for schengen, for security and for free movement in europe. there will be 10,000 border guards able to support member states with weaker borders or facing an emergency as we have seen recently with the refugee crisis. but, as i have always said, this reinforcement cannot be used to relieve the member states of their responsibilities. half of these 10,000 guards will come from the member states. are the member states prepared to meet their obligations? is portugal, my country, prepared to send 110 inspectors from the foreign and borders service as early as 2021? i am afraid that once again we are promising at european level what the states will not allow. the agency will also have a strengthened role in the management and enforcement of return decisions for persons who have no right to be present on the territory. today, incredibly, less than a third of the decisions actually enforced are those issued, excluding portugal, where no statistics on these decisions are known. finally, we shall also learn the extent of secondary movements in europe, that is, how many asylum seekers are actually fleeing their host country. it will finally be clear that five governments have been trampling on schengen and free movement for absolutely no reason. a final word to the rapporteur, mrs roberta metsola, for the exceptional work she has done in an absolutely record time.
the revision of the visa code is another element in the revision of european visa policy, but it has effects that go far beyond that. with this reform we are facilitating and promoting tourism, which is an essential sector for the european union and for portugal, and we are strengthening security through readmission agreements. we will reduce the bureaucracy associated with tourist visa applications, we will extend the collection network of periods and shorten the deadlines, thus making it faster and easier for tourists and frequent travellers to obtain a visa. in addition, taking into account the specificities of the cruise industry, which is so relevant to our outermost regions such as madeira and the azores, we have harmonised the rules for multiple entries, thus simplifying the lives of passengers who travel along our coast. on the other hand, we are also strengthening security in connection with the new union visa system, which i had the honour of reporting to you on, and we will be gathering more information and conducting more security checks before these visas are even issued. moreover, we have clearly linked visa policy with the readmission policy in the european union, so that we can reward the most cooperative states. i have no doubt, mr president, that this is a necessary and concrete step towards a more prosperous and more secure europe, and for that i congratulate mr lópez aguilar, mr claude moraes and all the other members of the libe committee.

mr president, the evidence of the toxicity of substances with endocrine disrupting activity goes back more than 20 years. the matter is made all the more serious when we consider that these substances circulate in our midst and are responsible for cases of infertility, obesity and cancer, both in humans and other species in our ecosystem. so far, there has been a case-by-case assessment based on an alert, and there is no truly transversal measure to end the presence of these substances. the case of bisphenol a in baby food has been highlighted in the media, but how many more of these substances do we have in contact with ourselves and our children through food, teaching material, toys or hygiene and cosmetic products? i voted in favour of this report because we need to look systematically at these substances and other substances of a similar nature which may be suspected of having hormonal destabilising activities. it is important to trigger the precautionary principle as there are many uncertainties about mechanisms of action, doses, acceptable exposures and combined effects of these substances. i would also like to make a final point about my total dedication in this mandate to health, the environment and human rights, which all seem to converge in this last plenary speech.

in writing. i acknowledge the compromise reached in the council on the top candidates. i think von der leyen is a strong candidate for the presidency of the european commission. i agree with his views on the common security and defence policy. his strength is certainly his knowledge of the baltic region. however, i see a problem with the agreement reached: there is no geographical imbalance in the distribution of top posts. neither central and eastern europe nor northern europe is represented.
the process of choosing the occupants of the top posts in the eu institutions is first and foremost an assertion of power by the directory of powers which has always determined the direction of the eu, namely the franco-german axis, dominated by germany, as the directory which decides and imposes. following the results of the european parliament elections, which showed an electoral erosion of the right-wing and social-democratic parties, members of the epp and s&d respectively, it became necessary to reconfigure the so-called 'brussels consensus' to include with another protagonism the so-called 'liberals', now formally necessary for the formation of the majorities necessary to enforce the continuity of the policies in force. the compromise reached confirms the deception of the so-called 'candidates for president of the european commission', a manoeuvre of political manipulation that sought to give a democratic appearance to a process that is undemocratic in its essence, while at the same time giving cover to the efforts to deepen the federalist character of the eu institutions and the capitalist integration process itself. the generally negative public perception of this process may lead its protagonists to contemporiza­tions, adjustments and punctual changes, or even a twist or a change, in the consensus now reached. but the intention is clear: beyond the characters of the moment, insist on the same policies.
the holding of elections is a major moment in democracies, allowing the will of voters to be expressed and bringing them closer to the political process, which is essential for the health of democracies and the legitimisation of institutions. in this respect, the 'spitzenkandidaten' process was a very positive development, increasing the transparency of political choices in the european union and conferring a stronger legitimacy on the european commission. the demonstrated inability to successfully complete the 'spitzenkandidaten' process was therefore quite negative for the necessary strengthening of the proximity between citizens and institutions. however, this should not distract us from what is most important to the citizens, which is the concrete policies that must be put in place to make the european union more just, cohesive and sustainable.
in writing. the main winners of the agreement on the new leaders of the european union are gender balance and alde/renew europe. the candidates for president of the european commission and president of the european central bank are women, and alde/re won the presidency of the european council. for the first time, alde/re was at the top of eu leadership. the geographical balance was not as good, as there were no representatives of eastern europe and the nordic countries in the top posts, and the european people's party had to withdraw from the spitzenkandidaten system, which it had strongly supported. but what is most important is, of course, the content of the european union's policy-making over the next five years.
the process leading to the outcome of the european council on the appointment of eu institutions' heads of state expressed contradictions inherent in the crisis of this process of capitalist integration, which did not break with the so-called 'brussels consensus' between the pse and the epp; but it has deepened it to include the so-called "liberals". the commitment and the protagonists found confirm the insistence on deepening federalist policies that challenge the sovereignty of the states; neoliberal policies that attack labour and social rights, public services and the social functions of the state; militarist and security policies, with authoritarian and anti-democratic expressions policies that feed the far right. the decisions of the european council prove the falsity and the manipulative nature of the electoral rhetoric about the 'social dimension', the 'social contract', the 'progressive alliances' or the 'democratic legitimacy' of the eu. it was once again the eu's power-directorate that dictated the political outcome of a process that will continue down a path that has already proved disastrous for workers and peoples. for our part, we will continue to defend, without compromise, the interests of the workers, the people and the country, presenting proposals that allow us to seize all the opportunities for the development of the country. we will be in the forefront of rejecting any measures or impositions that harm portugal.
mr president, i would like to say that the commitment, oral and written, by ms von der leyen, to more competitiveness, to a sustainable europe with more solidarity shows that she saw correctly our programme, but, ms von der leyen, you also saw the expectations of europeans. europe is in need of urgent action. so it was good to see that you didn’t hesitate in assuming commitments that go towards what citizens expect, which is clear commitment, clear achievements. we now expect due capacity to compromise and to overcome the blockages from the council on several issues: on carbonisation, on migration, on euro area reform, on the pillar of social rights. there’s no room for hesitation. huge challenges lie immediately on our hands, such as the approval of an adequate budget for europe. we take your words today, in your written words, with great expectation. parliament will deliver on its part. the citizens of the eu expect nothing less from us. that means that your pledge on this day is a solemn one. europeans demand nothing less than five years of redemption of the european project after 10 years of crisis and hesitation. let us rebuild from now on the europe of our dreams.

mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, saving lives is not, and cannot be, a crime. we cannot criminalize those who merely comply with the basic principles of human rights and the united nations convention on the law of the sea. it is shortsighted to imagine that refugees and migrants will stop seeking an opportunity for a life in safety and dignity if there is no one to help them. it is therefore necessary to revise the immigration facilitation directive, to revise the dublin rules, to develop a common asylum policy based on solidarity in sharing the responsibility for international protection, and to develop an integrated immigration policy based on legal and safe channels and a development cooperation policy capable of responding to the causes of migration. denying humanitarian aid, denying the dignity of every human being, denying the inalienable right to life is denying europe's humanist identity and is committing a crime against humanity.
mr. chairman, please! the fight for clean air is an absolutely very important issue and that is not something that anyone in this chamber is discussing, and that is not something that any of us are questioning. of course, the air in cities is especially polluted. what specifically can we do to improve air quality? let's focus on sustainable transport, on electric mobility, on improving the energy efficiency of buildings or on developing district heating in member states with low winter temperatures. but, gentlemen, to fight for something, you have to start with yourself. let's look at the workings of the european parliament. is it for environmental reasons that parliament is moving from brussels to strasbourg for three days? how we pollute the air by our travels! are these car lines in the parliament building waiting for meps polluting the air or are they irrelevant? maybe to make demands of others, you should start demanding of yourself. we use slogans, programs, restrictive targets, and yet we contribute to the air pollution, this time in strasbourg.
mr president, in her speech yesterday, ursula von der leyen committed herself to a climate neutral europe by 2050. this also means that low-emission zones will have to expand into european cities at a faster pace. low emission zones have been proven to provide a number of health benefits to urban dwellers, including reduced respiratory disease and mortality. these are therefore effective measures. unfortunately, they only partially solve the problem of highly polluting cars, by effectively displacing them to other urban areas. at the same time, they imply higher costs for individual cities in the form of more promoting public, pedestrian and cycling transport, or building catchment car parks and overall regulation of parking policy. however, cities in all the member states of the european union do not have sufficient resources in their budgets to cover these costs, and i would therefore like to ask that in the future we look for ways of finding resources to support, say, the faster construction of either catchment car parks or alternative transport options.
mr president, urban air quality zones are bringing some improvements in air quality and scientific studies confirm that they do indeed bring health benefits. but by creating these zones, mayors and councils are grappling with a problem that is much bigger than any city and goes beyond the borders of each of them. they are fighting because national governments, including the czech one, are failing to address air quality. at the same time, these zones are facing various difficulties that threaten their continued existence. one is that they become the subject of political struggle. it happens that a newly elected mayor stops collecting fines for violating the clean zone rules and his decision is subsequently challenged in court, which cancels his decision and orders the re-collection of these fines. for air quality, such an outcome is undoubtedly a win, but if these legal battles were to be repeated, the future of these zones could be highly uncertain. a particular weakness of the zones is that they are often politically contentious and therefore vulnerable. the systemic solution should be to tighten eu air legislation and ensure that car manufacturers bring their vehicles into compliance with existing emissions regulations. it is clear that we will not have really healthy air in our cities or at least hope for stabilizing the climate if there are a large number of cars running on fossil fuels on the roads of cities. so we need to gradually shift to electric vehicles and keep cars away from city centers.
mr president, commissioner, the air quality directive is fundamental to the lives of citizens and to the preservation of biodiversity. however, in portugal, the country where i was elected, there are several infringement procedures for failure to comply with this directive and what we see is that the portuguese government and the successive governments have not complied because they have targets that are not the most reliable for ensuring the pursuit of these objectives. and what we want is for you to really rethink not just the question of automobile mobility, but especially aviation-related mobility. in portugal, in lisbon, we have the airport in the city centre and an airport is being built, or is expected to be built, a few kilometres from the lisbon metropolitan area. it is essential that the european commission looks at this issue with an eye to the future and reforms this directive to make it more demanding and to enable member states to comply with it and, if they do not comply, to be pressured to do so.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, 9 out of 10 people live in environments with polluted air and 4 million die every year as a result of air pollution. that's the equivalent of the entire population of ireland disappearing every year. if you think this is a problem of developing countries or that it does not affect europe, you are wrong. every year in europe, as many people die prematurely from air quality problems as inhabitants of luxembourg. half a million people every year. and more and more europeans live in cities, where pollution is higher. pollution is making us sick: cancer, cognitive problems, dementia and diabetes are just part of the problem. and the data shows that even in rural areas, pollution limits are being exceeded. we need more control over air quality because what we can't measure, we can't improve. we need to work as a team because this is a team effort. here in parliament, it is urgent to decide and create mandatory solutions for cities on air quality control, planning and improvement. we need to create clean-emission zones in city centres and we need to make the air in our towns and our lives cleaner and healthier. this parliament was inaugurated a few weeks ago, but there is an urgent need to decide on this matter. it's measured by the lives we can save.
the resolution we are now discussing represents a clear statement in defence of human rights and the principles of democracy and the rule of law and a condemnation of their repeated violation by the authorities of the russian federation. we condemn the arbitrary detention of ukrainian citizens by russia on russian territory and in the temporarily occupied territories of ukraine and call for the immediate and unconditional release of the tatar activists, the ukrainians detained on politically motivated charges and the 24 sailors captured in the kerch strait. we condemn the attacks on the freedom of association and expression of civil society, including the law that leads to the classification of some ngos as foreign agents, and we strongly condemn the persecution of environmental activists. we call for the immediate release of these detainees, for the cessation of the use of disproportionate force against peaceful protesters and for the russian federation to return swiftly to the principles of the rule of law and respect for human rights.
on behalf of the ecr group. - madam president! - i have to go. this is obviously a very important resolution and the ecr group will support it. indeed, since 2014, i.e. since the illegal annexation of crimea and military aggression in eastern ukraine, we have witnessed many cases of human rights violations, disappearances, persecution and unlawful detention of ukrainians and other minorities. that's why we support sanctions. i would like to draw your attention to one thing. we all know that many member states are more inclined to protect their own commercial interests in russia than to actually comply with the sanctions. how, for example, could the austrian oil company omv have agreed with gazprom last month to buy its shares in the siberian gas fields for 900 million euros? how to explain that german direct investment in russia in 2019 recorded a 33 percent increase compared to the previous year? the eu's policy towards russia therefore needs to be more consistent.
the resolution is part of the escalation of confrontation between the united states and nato and the russian federation. an escalation of confrontation that includes the imposition of sanctions and that integrates the fascist-style coup d'état in ukraine in 2014, and with which the european union has actively aligned itself. the resolution conceals the policy of encirclement and bellicose warfare which the united states and nato have been pursuing against russia, particularly since 1999 with successive enlargements and the installation of military means along the borders of these countries. the resolution's whitewashing of the ukrainian regime and its policy of oppression and war against its own people, of the violence of the actions of ukrainian fascist groups against ukrainian populations, is particularly serious and significant. this situation brings further isolation, impoverishment and insecurity to the peoples of both regions, with consequences for other states. for example, as a result of the sanctions, portugal's rock pear producers lose around eur 250 million a year. a policy of peace and inclusion in a europe of the workers and peoples is a civilization's urgent need.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we all know that the treaties do not expressly mention the word 'forests'. we also know that in recent years the european institutions, the parliament and, of course, the commission, have been more active in managing this heritage. but i want to say here today that we believe that, despite the difficulties in managing a forest which is so different in the various countries, as agriculture is in so many countries that are part of the union, it has been possible to make a common agricultural policy. here too it must be possible to at least have a common forestry strategy. so we say yes to environmental conservation and ecological balance, we say yes to greater involvement of the municipalities, of the regions, we say yes to sustainable management of biomass, but we also say yes to the economic value of the forest. greater economic value of the forest with these associated aspects means more people in the territories, and more people in the territories means more balanced development. i would therefore like to make a suggestion, as my colleague simone schmiedtbauer has just said, concerning the strengthening of the solidarity fund. i would like to say, as we say in my country, that forest fires are not to be fought, they are to be avoided, and that is why i would like to leave the suggestion that we should have a fund to support forest prevention.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment and the environment from the adverse effects of chemical agents is a very good one. we are seeing the creation of genuine monopolies in the production processes in the agricultural sector. the multinationals control and divide the market among themselves at the expense of the ruin of many small and mediumsized farmers, the reduction of the diversity of production and the possibilities of choice of consumers, who have become more dependent on these multinationals. it is one thing to invent processes of genetic modification using molecular biology and intellectual property rights applied to those processes, and it is another thing to have the product, the living organisms, however new, that come from these technologies. the existence of intellectual property rights over these organisms is not acceptable, nor is the patenting of conventional genetic selection processes, crossbreeding processes or the resulting breeding products, such as new plant or animal varieties. nor are laboratory-assisted selection processes in which whole genomes are involved acceptable.

member of the commission. – madam president, i would like to thank honourable members for this important and timely debate. as a number of you have underlined, disinformation campaigns are designed to hamper the ability of our citizens to make informed choices, to divide our society and our communities, and to call into question the outcomes of our electoral processes and the integrity of our democratic institutions. i don’t think that we can accept this as a new normal. we must be ready to do more to counter it. part of the problem comes from outside of the eu. a number of you have mentioned russia. let me be clear: there is a team – it’s been referred to in the institutions – whose job it is to monitor pro-kremlin disinformation campaigns, and they report – this is publicly available – that, over a recent period when they checked, the amount of pro-kremlin coordinated disinformation across our member states had doubled compared with a year ago. so this is a real problem, and we should not duck that. let me also be clear that others have learned from the russian playbook, so we have to build our resilience – we have to build our counter-measures to resist and counter disinformation, wherever it comes from. that’s why we’ve put in place – from the commission’s side, working with the member states and with civil society – a series of measures. but i am not pleased, and we are not resting on our laurels: we say we need to do more. we need to do more on tackling online disinformation, and we need to do more on reinforcing the resilience of our elections. in the coming weeks and months, we will review the code of practice on disinformation to ensure that we can do better to protect the integrity of our democratic societies, and we will report to you on this by the end of october. let me just say, in response to one or two of the comments, that this code of practice on disinformation which we are pursuing with the social media platforms has absolutely nothing to do with censorship. it has absolutely nothing to do with the idea of a ministry of truth. it is not about determining that a particular political comment is good or bad, true or false. it is about shining a light on the provenance of political debate. it is about transparency, so that our citizens know when they see information where it comes from, who’s paying for it, and whether it’s a human or an artificial machine that is circulating that information. i think that is essential, if we’re going to be able to equip our citizens to make good choices. we will also come back to you to report on the conduct of the european parliament elections and the implementation of the election package of measures on free and fair elections. again, we will work on a european democracy action plan to build the resilience of our democratic systems, and we expect to put forward – subject to confirmation – legislative proposals to ensure greater transparency on paid political advertising and clearer rules on the financing of european political parties. alongside this, at the same time, we will continue to work with fact—checkers, researchers and civil society, as well as the member states and online platforms, so that together we can make the progress we need to ensure a prosperous, safe and democratic future for europe and our citizens.
president-in-office of the council. – madam president, thank you for the opportunity to participate in this debate. it is on a very topical issue and one which is a priority for our presidency. i would like to thank members for their very active participation. the resilience of our union is based on unity. the more united we are, the better we can withstand the spread of disinformation. it is our obligation to spare no effort to protect our elections and more generally our democratic systems from foreign interference, and we have to do it in full respect of fundamental rights such as freedom of speech, the media and association. we will continue working horizontally across sectors, across borders, learning from each other through the exchange of information and best practices. and progress has been made. however, as commissioner jourová and commissioner king and many of you here said, we must remain vigilant, and as i reiterated, the presidency has placed significant emphasis on hybrid threats. we also look forward to the new initiatives announced by the commission. and let me once again point out that general awareness of the hybrid threats among our citizens is essential. while open societies and public administrations provide correct information, there won’t be lasting room for disinformation. so thank you again for this interesting and important debate.

in writing. the european union's (eu) obsession with the possible interference of third countries in the democratic processes of its member states is above all an exercise in cynicism, when it is known that the eu itself is a constant source of interference in the sovereignty of other countries. it is not surprising, therefore, that this resolution is full of unsubstantiated accusations and full of support for organisations aiming at destabilising third countries, controlling the personal data and information of citizens, and asserting the military and geo-strategic interests of an eu which is increasingly concerned with markets rather than democracy. fake news, the quality of information and information literacy should be issues of concern to all of us. but we have to recognize that it is often the eu and the european institutions that promote fake news. confronting this problem is not about inventing an external enemy; the democratization of the media, the end of the concentration of the media in large groups, the end of the submission of content to the interests of the large economic groups, the strengthening of the public media, the enhancement of the working conditions of the professionals in the sector.

we know that cancer is currently the second leading cause of death in the european union and that in 2018 there were 3 million new diagnoses. cancer thus has a major impact on health systems, but also on the european economy. 30 to 50% of these cases could be avoided by reducing risk factors and implementing evidence-based preventive strategies. the current investment of the european union is insufficient to achieve the objectives we have set ourselves. increased investment is needed in primary and primary prevention through policies that promote better lifestyles and healthy environments, as well as in the early detection of cancer. to this end, it is essential to support scientific research with sound studies, such as cohort studies, so that we can base our decisions on the best possible scientific evidence.
cancer is one of the leading causes of suffering and death in europe. in recent decades, however, there have been important scientific advances in the ability to cure a significant proportion of neoplastic diseases and in the possibility of treating the majority of patients, minimising the impact of cancer and promoting improvements in quality of life and survival time. at the same time, treatments that alleviate suffering became available and there was widespread recognition of the importance of palliative care, allowing those who are struck by more aggressive and extreme forms of cancer to live with dignity. in the face of the impact of the disease and the increased possibilities for response, european citizens have reason to expect more from the european union. there are three priorities to be pursued. firstly, the fight against the preventable causes of cancer, the main cause of which is still tobacco smoke. then, the creation of a system that makes it easy for everyone to access the most modern and effective treatments. finally, support for research at all levels, which will enable us to know more and better fight this disease.

mr. speaker, facebook has decided to create its own cryptocurrency, libra, with 28 partners including visa and mastercard as well as the internet platforms uber and booking. the libra association's board of directors will be appointed in geneva on 14 october. there are three dangers that come with creating this cryptocurrency. firstly, it poses a risk to the stability of the financial system and to monetary sovereignty. with more than 2 billion users, facebook will bring tough competition to national currencies, as well as to the euro, especially since the pound is completely flexible in use since it is not governed by any monetary and financial code. secondly, this flexibility will also make the pound an ideal instrument for money laundering, as the us central bank has pointed out. finally, the third danger is that the libra will not offer any reliable protection of users' personal data. for these three reasons, i warn the european parliament against the complacency of mrs lagarde, the president of the ecb, in relation to this new currency. we must fight the pound, which is nothing but a private currency seeking to supplant public currencies.

mr president, commissioner, the european union has shown over the years strong signs of solidarity with the different member states, and there are many examples i could list here, but i will concentrate on what really matters, the people. during the passage of hurricane lorenzo in the azores, an outermost region, on 2 october this year, some 255 incidents were recorded and 53 people had to be relocated. the passage of this hurricane caused the total destruction of the port of lajes in flores, which put at risk the supply to the western group of the azores archipelago. according to the regional government of the azores, the damage caused by the hurricane is estimated at eur 330 million. mr president, i call on the european commission once again to show that we are stronger together. it's time to show that we're ruling for the people and that we're attentive to their needs. i therefore call for the solidarity fund to be mobilised quickly and for the funds to be reprogrammed to help the people of the azores cope with the enormous losses.
mr president, mr commissioner, we have all spoken here of solidarity, in this case with spain and the autonomous region of the azores, an outermost region, and we all think that the word solidarity is a very dear word in all european policies. we have just heard the commissioner give us a number of perspectives from which this solidarity can be put into practice. i sincerely believe that, as has been said just now by isabel and josé manuel fernandes and others, what is at stake here is not just the list of hypotheses, but putting those hypotheses into practice. and what i find is that there are, in some cases or in some situations, the surveys made, in others not even that, but populations and companies and people, as has been mentioned, people who have unfortunately disappeared, grieving families, but also people displaced, without property, without equipment, with economic life destroyed. and if there is a moment when this solidarity fund, in good time created and adjusted, in addition to the other mechanisms, but tend to be put on the ground at the right time. the president of the portuguese republic said yesterday that this matter must never be forgotten. we will not let it fall into oblivion, and that is our appeal to the commission.
mr president, when hurricane lorenzo struck the azores, it caused enormous material damage, but it should be noted that, fortunately, there was no personal damage. this is thanks to the action of the civil protection of the regional government of the azores and the enormous awareness of the azoreans when they are faced with a hurricane of that intensity. the most serious damage, as has already been said, is that to the port of lajes de flores, whose operation is essential for the supply of all kinds of essential goods to the two islands, the populations of flores and corvo. this requires a rapid response, which is why portuguese s&d meps sent a question to the european commission as early as 3 october. the s&d group members did not come to this issue twenty days later for reasons of internal party guerrilla warfare. we understand that we have to be all united to find answers to people's problems. these replies go through the solidarity fund, but, commissioner, 2.5 per cent of support is not enough for 330 million in damages. we need more, much more european solidarity for the azoreans and we will be here to remind you that this must deserve a response from the european union.
the commission has also proposed that the european parliament and the council adopt a common position on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council amending regulation (ec) no 1260/1999 as regards the definition of the concept of 'originating products' and methods of administrative cooperation. the previous action plan had ended at the end of 2016 and this was followed by a prolonged silence that lasts until today. the truth is that it is a grave mistake to downplay this epidemic. there are more than 800 000 europeans living with hiv infection. 120,000 of them don't know they're carrying the virus. year after year there are 25,000 new cases diagnosed, and that number has not decreased. to combat the hiv/aids epidemic we need to keep this issue at the centre of political attention and we need an appropriate strategy from the european union. to give up is a very serious mistake and the european parliament, on behalf of the citizens of europe, has an obligation to insist that the new european commission resumes an action plan on this matter.

madam president, this is one of those moments that doesn't allow for half-words or silences. the turkish military offensive in north-east syria is a serious violation of international law, threatens the already fragile stability and security in the region, has led to the forced displacement of populations weakened by a long conflict and has opened the door to a possible resurgence of daesh. it cannot pass without a firm condemnation. the european council's initiative to impose an arms embargo, while welcome, is not enough. it should extend to all ongoing supplies and go further by adopting sanctions targeting individuals directly or indirectly involved in violations of human rights and economic interests. it must be made very clear that the european union will not be blackmailed, that we will continue to provide humanitarian support to syrian refugees and to the neighbouring countries that are hosting them, but we reject any forced return operation. the return of these citizens to their country can only be done of their own free will and once conditions are created for them to do so in dignity and safety. the european union cannot support any social reengineering operation. in this difficult moment we cannot fail to highlight the courage of the kurdish people and the democratic forces of syria in fighting alongside the international coalition against daesh and in drawing the appropriate consequences from this, while maintaining our solidarity.

madam president, sometimes we need to explain why we need to do something. but not today. today, our businesses, our cities and our people ask us and want us to act to stop global warming, to stop deforestation, to stop climate change. climate change will not slow down because of single actions, because climate change knows no borders. from china to the us, australia and many places in the world, climate change affects us all. we can only win if we work together, leaving no one behind. we must act on coal. we must act on aviation fuel tax exemptions. fossil fuels belong to the past and together we must look into the future of energy. it’s time to let go of old technologies. it’s time to create and fund new ones. let’s push europe forward. let’s invest more in science and innovation. let’s make trade deals that fulfil the paris agreement. we face that choice. either we act together or climate will change us all.
in writing. climate change requires an effective response by the commission that accompanies the recognised urgency of the situation. on the basis of the available scientific data, in line with the climate change already observed, such as sea level rise and increased forest fires, among other calamities, decisive action is needed to allow for a clear decarbonisation. the commission will have to show commitment on this issue and will have to allocate resources to environmental issues in order to achieve the targets set for 2030 and 2050. the member states must also commit themselves to complying with the legislation produced by the european union, to strengthening the adoption of more ambitious plans in line with the 2050 objectives and to implementing the plans approved by each state.

mr. president, primary health care is the most efficient and cost-effective way for us to achieve universal health coverage. but to achieve the 2030 sustainable development goals, we need to accelerate the pace. when we look at the concentration of human resources in health, it's high in large urban areas, causing a shortage of resources in more remote areas. madeira and porto santo, as islands in an ultra-peripheral region, are examples of this reality, where only 64% of the inhabitants have a family doctor assigned to them, leaving a third of the population without a doctor responsible for managing their health. we need mechanisms from the european union to support member states in creating and implementing health-promoting policies, with increased investment in all areas of health, from socioeconomic and environmental determinants to health care delivery services. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's macro-financial assistance in the context of the union's macro-financial assistance to the countries of central and eastern europe. and since health is ultimately a political choice, we can make that decision. if we don't invest in health, we're divesting from our society. (the speaker accepts to answer a question under the 'blue card' procedure (rule 171 (8)))
blue-card answer. – thank you, colleague, for your question. it is important that member states receive the necessary support to tackle these constraints they have in providing health care. and human resources for health is, without a doubt, one of the main problems we have that will allow citizens to receive and get access to proper healthcare. if we have remote areas that don’t have the proper human resources for health, that means our citizens are not getting access to the healthcare they deserve.

mr president, the convention on the rights of the child states in article 19 that states must take all measures to protect the child against all forms of physical or mental violence, injury or abuse, neglect or wanton treatment, ill-treatment or exploitation, including sexual abuse. however, according to the 2018 report on human trafficking within the european union, 20,532 victims were recorded between 2015 and 2016, of which 23% were children. we are talking about 4,723 children who have been exploited by human trafficking networks, and more than half of them have been trafficked for sexual exploitation. and here we cannot fail to stress that women are the most affected. these are truly alarming figures. although there is already a strategy to eradicate trafficking in human beings in europe, we need to redouble our efforts to ensure that this scourge is also effectively combated in the member states, by severely punishing those responsible and, above all, by protecting the victims.

millions of people have taken to the streets in chile and have resisted the violent repression by the police and military forces under the government of president piñera. in this way, in two weeks, they have brought down the recurring fallacy of the "exemplary chilean economic model", which was implemented from 1973 onwards with the imposition of a brutal and bloody fascist dictatorship in chile, whose neoliberal policies translate, among other serious aspects, into a brutal intensification of exploitation and the destruction of the central role of the state in ensuring democratic rights (social, economic, cultural), in particular by privatizing virtually all spheres of economic life, public services (health, education, transport) and social security, handing them over to large monopoly groups. chile has become, in recent decades, one of the most unequal countries in the world, and the chilean people are in the streets demanding a change of policy and the end of the constitutional regime that supports them. they deserve our full solidarity.
in writing. there are policies that unite us and policies that divide us. but in the case of the emergency that is everyone's fight against climate change, i think, with a few honorable exceptions, it is much more what unites us than what divides us. i have no great doubt, therefore, that at the end of the day the european parliament will declare, by a clear vote, the climate emergency: reduce emissions by 55% in general, reduce emissions from the maritime and aviation sectors, create a fund to mitigate the economic and social effects of these changes. this position we are preparing to take is, some might say, a declaratory one. i might. but it's a sign of political commitment that we give to the world. it's a warning we've added to many others on the eve of the un climate conference. we're going to declare a climate emergency. now we're making ourselves heard. tomorrow we will act. we have to act.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, violence against women is something that must be condemned in the strongest terms. i personally believe that any man who touches a woman is a scoundrel who deserves severe punishment. but that's not going to happen through this document. and it's not going to happen through this document, because it's not designed to save women from violence. i've heard hundreds of names of victims of domestic violence, and they were from countries that have ratified, signed this convention. what did she protect them with? how did you help them? no, it didn't. why would you do that? because this document has another purpose and another task. this document mixes the legitimate issue of women's rights with the absurd and controversial lgbtqi... the latter to close the door, propaganda, same-sex marriage, social gender roles. i may surprise you, but gender is not a social construct. he is biology. it may surprise you, but there are countries in europe that still believe that the family is a voluntary marriage between a man, a woman and their children, and we don't want that to change. i'm from bulgaria, we are against this convention, we have not signed it and we will not sign it.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, italy is one of the countries which has signed and ratified the istanbul convention, and yet the figures are alarming: 142 women were killed in 2018, 94 in the first 10 months of 2019, 14,000 complaints of stalking, 17,000 complaints of mistreatment. but the situation in the other countries of the european union is no better. it's 2019, and gender violence comes in many forms, not just physical, not just psychological. gender inequality is also wage inequality: you think women have to work 66 more days to earn as much as men. what do these figures mean? that more effort is needed, that the convention must be properly applied, otherwise there are only fine words on a piece of paper. in italy we have now made a great step forward with the code red law, but more steps still have to be taken and here in europe we must show the way. we need to invest more in education, we need to fund anti-violence centres all over europe and above all we need to talk about it, talk about it all the time and not just on 25 november.
